# MANIFESTS — apply order and verification contract

**What this file answers:** in what order the blocks apply, what "verified" means for each one, and what to
do when an apply fails.  The current release identity (fork point, tip/tree, block count, per-artifact
sha256) is [`release.json`](release.json); the dated record of every release and re-validation is
[`WORKLOG.md`](WORKLOG.md).  Dated validation records from the pre-V2.0 era (the 16-block set, its
per-block table and its release-by-release narrative) are preserved in
[`archive/docs/validation-history.md`](archive/docs/validation-history.md) and do **not** describe the
current delivery.

## Current delivery

**18 official blocks (`0000`-`0017`) + the temporary block `0300` = 19 default patches** against
`release.json.base`, canonical chain `release.json.tip` / `.tree` (`release.json.n_default` is the apply
count; `release.json.n_blocks` is the **official** count that names the `v<N>` tag).  Block 17 is the
TQ1_0 / TQ2_0 (ternary) GPU path from r356 — the number was the two-arm server's until it was withdrawn at
r352, and that server is still in `beta/prefill-decode-arms/`.  The optional blocks
(`optional/*/01xx-*.patch`: `0100` two-lane prefill, `0101` mixed K/V) are **not** part of this set:
`--include-optional` applies them on top, in numeric order, and the checkout is then not the release tree.
`scripts/validate-set.sh` is green (checksums and recorded counts, then the strict default apply on a
fresh tarball (19/19, strict), tree equality, a conflict-marker/subject scan of every block tree, the optional artifacts
applied strictly and tree-checked, the Windows portability lint of the files the set adds — issue
#231 — then the **file-set reconciliation** of every block against the release it replaces, `TODO` #358,
which is what catches a re-base that landed fewer files than the patch it replaces carried — checks 1-9).
The optional **build** is not in CI: `scripts/gate-optional-build.sh` (gate 47) compiles the cumulative
optional-applied tree on a GPU box, once per release (issue #371).
**The two-arm server (block 17 until r352) is WITHDRAWN from the delivery** and lives in
`beta/prefill-decode-arms/` (apply with `--include-beta`); it is not in `patches/`, not counted in `v<N>`,
and its gates (`gate-arms-spec.sh`, `gate-draft-ctx.sh`, `gate-arms-soak.sh`, and gate 17's TIER rows) are
**beta-only**, not release gates.  **The artifact in that directory was REPLACED on 2026-11-24 by the v2 design**
(ONE `llama_memory_i`, TWO `llama_context`s: the prefill arm owns the memory, the decode arm borrows it; see the
row at the end of the block table): the `scripts/gate-arms-*.sh` names above are the **OLD** artifact's gates,
while the v2 candidate's own gates live in `wip/prefill-decode-arms-v2/gates/`, and neither set is a release gate.
**Block `0300` is temporary** (`-sm relaxed-tensor`, a local relaxation of an upstream rule, not a
supported feature) -- it is applied last, kept separate, and expected to be dropped.  Each block is
**one** `git am` commit and the series is **linear**: apply in order, never reorder, never insert.

| 13 | `0013-…-block-13-MTP-draft-vocabulary-subset-…` | MTP draft vocabulary subset | **opt-in** (`--spec-draft-mtp-vocab N`), default off; draft-only |
| 14 | `0014-…-block-14-the-streamed-KV-a-fixed-VRAM-re` | **streamed KV** (**opt-in**, `--kv-window <cells>\|auto`; `LLAMA_KV_RESIDENT_CELLS` = deprecated alias) | the KV's VRAM cost is a resident window of the newest `W` cells, the rest in pinned system RAM in ONE VA reservation (no kernel or op change); the window slides in whole map units and `--fit` sizes it; **since r204 it also runs on a tensor split** (one region PER CHILD device, all children at the same `W` cells, the grow fanned out to every child; `--fit` charges the physical `W x cell_bytes` against the split's per-device budget; a MIRRORED dsv4/HRM cache declines it) -- before r204 `-sm tensor` declined it (r181); **r289 (issue #314): the spill boundary is a CELL COUNT taken from the USED extent and turned into bytes per tensor**, because an uneven `-ts` ratio gives two tensors of one cache different cell strides (a shared byte boundary short-mapped the shorter tensors — a GPU write fault — and asked past the region end on the last), and because the padded `n_kv` must not decide what spills (`LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_PREFILL` still sets the MAPPED extent, so VRAM is `W + pad` cells at most); the cost model calibrates **every** child and quotes `max_i(share_i x bytes / rate_i)` (the children read in parallel), the refusal log names the reason that failed (the map cap only when configured), and `-sm layer` is the recommended split on a heterogeneous or slow-link set; `LLAMA_KV_SPILL_RAM_PCT` caps the pinned spill; + r190: a cache that is **streaming** at the depth it reads takes the **sparse** QSA decode arm (`LLAMA_QSA_DENSE_DECODE_STREAM=0` is the A/B arm)  **+ r190: the ATTENDANCE POOL** (`--kv-attendance-pool [on|off]`, alias `--kv-region-pool`; **off by default**, and **since r247 not a size knob**: the pool is derived from the model, one decode band of the indexer working set, and a legacy numeric value is accepted-and-ignored) — the KV's authoritative copy in a pinned host **mirror** plus a compact **device pool** of indexer-block pages and a page table, resolved by `GGML_OP_KV_POOL` (mark / claim / map+fill) with the QSA reader taking a second id list; `--fit` books the mirror as host RAM (only the pool is VRAM) and does not interpolate `-c` while the pool is active.  A fittability feature like the window: **default off**, unset = byte-identical including the OOM, and mutually exclusive with the window.  **+ r193: the MTP DRAFT WINDOW** (`--spec-draft-kv-window`, **default `0` since r247** — a window the request outgrows cost −9.7 % of MTP decode on a `-sm tensor` split at ~105k tokens, `benchmarks/2026-11-10-mtp-draft-window-depth.md`; `auto` = *the cap or nothing*: 65536 cells, or no window when that would not leave a batch of room below every sequence's own context; `N` = cells; the r193 sizer was a quarter of the context clamped to [32768, 65536]) — the draft context's KV is a window instead of `-c` cells, and an embedded MTP head's slab draft reserve is derived with the window (aux 3.50 -> 0.76 GiB, arena +2.8 GiB); MTP only and embedded-head only (a separate MTP draft GGUF keeps its full cache); **+ r248: the window is a TRUE ring** — a batch whose positions do not advance (an M-RoPE image chunk puts every row on one temporal position) evicts the drafter's oldest cells instead of running the `win + one batch` spare room out and failing the request (`failed to find a memory slot` → HTTP 500 `failed to process mtmd chunk`, issue #297) — and **a drafter failure can no longer fail a request**: it drops the draft KV and retries once, then disables drafting for that sequence until the next request |
| 15 | `0015-…-block-15-the-UMA-GTT-expert-store-an-APU` | **the UMA GTT expert store** (an integrated GPU's expert set in ONE registered, `mlock`ed region; **sized by `-lme N|auto`**, which is also the opt-in for STREAMING) | an APU has no host-expert tier (r236) and upstream disables the expert cache there, so a MoE set larger than RAM could not run: the store is ONE anonymous region (`mmap` + `MADV_HUGEPAGE` + one `hipHostRegister(Mapped\|ReadOnly)`, `mlock`ed, sliced per `(tensor, expert)`) read in place at 230-233 GB/s.  **The shape follows the budget**: a budget that covers the whole set PLACES it (static, no eviction); a budget below it takes the **ARENA** (the store is the cache's slot storage, the expert tensors are holes in the read-only model mapping, LFRU residency serves every read, a miss is one buffered `pread` into the vacated slot).  `-lme` is also the streaming opt-in: a >RAM model runs on whatever `RLIMIT_MEMLOCK` allows, and one whose weights do not fit RAM is refused at load (0.2 s, with the fix).  The MTP/nextn head is pinned outside the budget and never evicted; one store is shared safely by two models; the fills run on block 12's adaptive transport (the buffered arm reads straight into the slot); on RDNA3_5 UMA the ladder starts on that arm; prefill streams only what is not resident, coalesced and in parallel.  **Inert on a discrete GPU by measurement.**  **r344 (issue #347 follow-up): a PINNED `map` transport on the arena is NAMED** -- the tier's memory-tight warning required `planned_pool > 0`, and the arena has no host pool, so `--load-mode mmap` ran silently; it now fires for the arena, names the budget/set and points at `buffered`/`auto`.  `dio` is valid on the arena too (the arena force-maps the model, so the lazy PLE stays mapped) and is a zero-churn, drive-dependent alternative (`ENVIRONMENT.md` §20).  Since r263 |
| 17 | `0017-…-block-17-TQ1_0-TQ2_0-ternary-weights-on-` | **TQ1_0 / TQ2_0 (ternary) weights on the GPU** — `MUL_MAT` (not `MUL_MAT_ID`), `GET_ROWS`, the fp16/fp32/bf16 dequantise rows, the decode fusions and the `graph_optimize` reorder; bit-identical to the CPU backend's x86 AVX2 path (contributor PR #358, r356) | default-on; `GGML_CUDA_TQ=0` refuses the types again (the CPU backend runs them), `GGML_CUDA_TQ_FUSE=0` takes the fusions away, `GGML_CUDA_TQ_CENSUS=1` censuses the launches.  Gate: `scripts/gate-tq.sh` (gate 42).  Nothing outside `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/` is touched, and a non-TQ model is untouched (text + rate measured) |
| — | *(withdrawn)* | **the two-arm server — was block 17 until r352; its candidate was REPLACED 2026-11-24 (v2 design)** | not in `patches/` and no block exists.  The artifact is **TWO patch files under `beta/prefill-decode-arms/`**, applied in filename order by `apply-all.sh --include-beta`: `prefill-decode-arms.patch` (block 17 itself — **sha256 `a2badad1…e366fe3c`, 42 files, +3073/−741**, cut against this release's tree `27db9bfd4`) then `state-split-2d-read.patch` (**STEP 15, a DELIVERY-level fix the candidate carries until it is cut into the delivery as a block-09 amendment — sha256 `b3f0645a…c2a7336b`, 1 file, +85/−46**, cut on top of patch 1 because it edits `ggml-cuda.cu`, which block 17 also edits), and then `two-arm-drop-boundary.patch` (**STEP 16, likewise delivery-level — sha256 `fd50d736…d31030963`, 4 files, +97/−4**: the pair keeps the prefill→decode compute-buffer drop, fenced by the other arm's step boundary, which restores CUDA graph capture and un-halves uncontested decode), the three applying to tree **`c0c261e45`** (it *replaces* the r346-r352 artifact whole: the old design's second context that viewed and co-owned the first's memory is gone at the root — ONE `llama_memory_i`, TWO `llama_context`s, the prefill arm owns and the decode arm borrows).  Applied only by `scripts/apply-all.sh --include-beta`.  Its gates are beta-only, never delivery gates; a promotion candidate has no row here until the maintainer admits it as block 17.  See `beta/prefill-decode-arms/README.md` (what it is, the measured trade, the residuals, the shapes it refuses), `beta/prefill-decode-arms/BETA-TESTING.md` (the tester's ladder) and the campaign record `wip/prefill-decode-arms-v2/` |
| 0300 | `0300-…-block-0300-relaxed-tensor-split-mode-TEM` | `-sm relaxed-tensor` (TEMPORARY, **in the default apply but not an official block**) | a local relaxation of upstream #31081: tensor split with a per-device MoE amax scalar, for `flint`/`mellum`/`mistral4`.  **Not a supported feature**; delete this patch to return to the r136 tree |

`rdna-boosts-all.patch` (repo root) is the entire net as ONE patch.  It is a convenience for a one-off
build at the fork point; it is **not** the reviewable increment and not a substitute for the per-block
flow.

## Apply (fresh checkout at the fork point)

```bash
git clone https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp && cd llama.cpp
git checkout "$(jq -r .base /path/to/rdna-boosts/release.json)"
bash /path/to/rdna-boosts/scripts/apply-all.sh .     # strict 18/18 default git am, tree checked vs release.json
# optional blocks 0100/0101 on top (NOT the release tree): --include-optional / --include-optional-all
```

`scripts/apply-all.sh` is the only supported flow.  **Do not `git apply` the concatenated series** — it
silently drops hunks (measured: 30 files / 2483 lines instead of 35 / 6094).  The script:

1. refuses a dirty tree or an existing `rdna-boosts` branch;
2. applies the whole series with strict `git am` (one commit per block, original subjects);
3. on failure, aborts and retries the series **once** with `git am -3` (3-way against the recorded blob
   ids), warning that the merged tree may differ from the canonical one;
4. on the strict path, asserts the applied tree equals `release.json.tree` — a stale fork point or a
   partially regenerated patch set fails here — and prints the applied **tip** (`release.json.tip`, the
   commit the personal fork's `rdna-boosts` branch carries).  `apply-all.sh` pins the committer
   identity/date, so a re-cut on a real checkout of `.base` reproduces that tip; `validate-set.sh`
   itself cannot compare it (it builds `.base` from the commit tarball, so its commit ids differ by
   construction) — `scripts/fork-refresh.sh` does that comparison.  The `.tip` rule is in `AGENTS.md`.
   partially regenerated set fails here rather than at the build;
5. `--include-optional` then steps through `optional/*/01xx-*.patch` in **numeric block order** (`0100`
   before `0101`, not directory order) on top of the *verified* tree and asks `[y/N]` for each one (`N` is
   the default), applying the accepted ones with strict `git am`, one commit each (an optional artifact is
   cut against exactly this tree, so the script refuses a 3-way merge); `--include-optional-all` applies
   every one without asking, which is what a scripted or headless apply must use.  `--include-beta` then
   applies `beta/*/*.patch` the same way.  Either flag leaves the checkout **not** the release tree, which
   the script reports; optional artifacts are not covered by the gates below (`optional/README.md`).
   **What IS covered since r285: every `optional/*/01xx-*.patch` must still apply strictly on the default
   tree** -- `validate-set.sh`'s check 8 applies each one alone (and compares its tree to
   `release.json.optional`), then all of them in numeric order (and compares
   `release.json.optional_tree`), on the freshly built default tree, and fails when one has drifted
   (`TODO` #324: both artifacts had gone stale for 40+ releases because "rebase it every release" was a
   policy with no instrument).
   **And since r367 the cumulative tree must BUILD**: `scripts/gate-optional-build.sh` (gate 47) is a release
   gate, because a tree that applies and re-verifies is not a tree that compiles -- issue #371 shipped a
   non-compiling `0101` tree through r360-r366 with check 8 green.

## Release gates (all mandatory, recorded in the release's `WORKLOG.md` entry)

**Where the gates run.**  There are three tiers, and which one a change hits is what keeps the cheap gate
cheap:

| tier | where | what | trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `.github/workflows/validate.yml` (hosted, no GPU) | `validate-set.sh`: strict apply on a fresh tarball, tree/count, per-artifact sha256, the Windows path + portability lints, a conflict-marker/subject scan of every block tree, and the file-set reconciliation when the clone has the previous release's tag | every push and PR, ~20 s |
| 2 | `radiance` (`scripts/validate-gpu.sh`, cron every 30 min) | re-applies the set at `origin/main` from scratch, **reconciles every block against the release it replaces**, builds the **whole applied tree once**, the seven op oracles, then `gate-prefill-logits.sh` (records a base on a fresh box, compares after) | every new `origin/main` commit, unattended |
| 3 | a GPU box, by hand | the release flow: `all` build, coherence, MTP Protocol A, arch sweeps, the generated-docs drift check (`scripts/check-gen-docs.sh`, r329) and the optional-artifact build (`scripts/gate-optional-build.sh`, gate 47, r367) | before a tag |

Tier 2 exists because almost every block is a CUDA/HIP kernel change and a hosted runner has no ROCm, so
tier 1 cannot compile any of it.  It is the automation behind "a re-base is a decode AND prefill change"
(`INVARIANTS.md`).  It holds its own `flock` and **skips while a container build holds the watcher's lock**,
so the two worker jobs never contend; summaries land in `~/.cache/rdna-validate/runs/<sha>/summary.txt`, and
a rev is retried at most `MAX_ATTEMPTS` times (3) before the script stops and names the `failed-<sha>` file
to remove.  The release container images are built **off-host by the worker** and are not a CI tier
([`CONTAINERS.md`](CONTAINERS.md)).

**A re-base owes every block's per-block gate.**  Re-expressing the set touches all 18 blocks at once, so the
per-block gate table below is owed **in full**, not only for the blocks whose text was edited.  The r328 re-base
ran the ladder, the coherence arms, the prefill point and the block gates it remembered and **missed block 00's
`gate-glu-inplace.sh`**, which is how that block's `ggml-alloc.c` half stayed dropped for two releases while its
docs still described it (r331, issue #345, `WORKLOG.md` 261121.010).  The mechanical warning for that class is now
an **instrument**, not a habit (**gate 38**): a build only proves that the tree *compiles*, and a missing
file's hunks inside other files compile fine, so `scripts/check-rung-file-sets.py` reconciles every block's
change against the block it replaces (file, `#define`, env var, option and the block itself) and fails on
anything the previous release carried and the new set does not.  Run it **per rung while the ladder is being
climbed** — that is when a lost file is a five-minute fix — and again before the tag.

**Gate 38 reconciles inventory, not needs, so a second instrument covers the moved-include class (r342).**
`scripts/check-system-headers.py` (**check 10** of `validate-set.sh`) fails when a line the delivery inserts
calls a symbol from a system header the file's include closure does not reach, and when any such line guards an
`#if` on that macro.  The r328 re-base moved `server_prompt_cache::take()` to a NEW file and left
`<sys/mman.h>` behind, so `#if defined(__linux__) && defined(MADV_HUGEPAGE)` dropped the huge-page branch out of
every Linux build and `LLAMA_SERVER_CACHE_HUGEPAGE` did nothing for twelve releases (issue #348,
`INVARIANTS.md` r342).  That is invisible to gate 38 (no file, define, env var or option disappeared: the include
was still there, in the file the code had left) and to any build.  Its negative control is the r339 tree, which
reports exactly the two `server-slot-sched.cpp` sites.  The **rule** it enforces is the one to carry into review:
guard the OS, never the macro a header declares.

`scripts/validate-set.sh` covers patch integrity only (checksums + a fresh-tarball strict apply) and runs
in CI.  The model-level gates are manual:

1. **Coherence** — same-seed `llama-cli` output identical to a known-good build (dense and, for a change
   that touches the MoE path, a host-expert config).  A host-expert **text arm has an oracle** since r310: pin
   the residency prior and the BLAS library (`MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`, `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` — the heat map
   now ships next to the model and the two together make the shape deterministic over 42 runs in twelve states,
   `benchmarks/2026-11-18-host-expert-prefill-oracle.md`), and **report repeats as a spread, not one hash**:
   `scripts/gate-host-expert-text.sh` IS that arm — N repeats, any spread fails, an unpinned invocation is
   refused, the flipped-library arm is printed as evidence, and `--kld` chains the prefill-logit point.  If a
   spread reappears, compare the heat map's mode and `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT` before blaming the change
   (`INVARIANTS.md` r310).  `GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=nccl` is not a bit-identical
   reference under `-sm tensor`; treat it as a smoke comparison.  **A change to the host-expert scheduler hook
   (`ggml_backend_sched_copy_input`'s routing paths, or a `moe_cache_remap`/`_gather`/`_update` iface member)
   owes both kill-switch arms** (`GGML_MOE_DEVREMAP=0`, `MOE_HOST_POOL_COLD=0`, `--host-experts pool`) plus a
   sequentially driven 2-slot server arm, and **never** a *concurrent* two-slot text diff as the oracle
   (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §38/§82: the unchanged eager build reads two text basins run to run on this box).  Since
   r221 a remap arm also owes **the exit report's `device remap: N tables served` line** -- a member that is
   never offered, or always declined, measures nothing (`INVARIANTS.md` r221 fact 6).  **A
   change that touches a cache-*quantizer*
   fusion also needs a `-ctk/-ctv q8_0` arm** (the fusions key on the q8_0 `SET_ROWS` and are inert on an f16
   cache: r187's five folds are worth +13.4 % tg128 on that arm and nothing at all at f16), on **1 and 2+
   device** `-sm tensor`/`-sm layer` arms — the submitter's own r187 matrix stopped at one card.

   **1b. A LoRA's delta must survive the fused chain (r318, GitHub issue #331) — `scripts/gate-lora-fusion.sh`.**
   A fusion that replaces a node's output (the Q8_1 MUL fusion writes only the matmul's Q8_1 activation and never
   the F32 MUL) is safe only if the fused chain owns **every** reader, and the only reader count that matters is
   the view-aware one: `build_lora_mm()` adds a `MUL_MAT` on the same activation, and it reaches the MUL through
   the same reshape the matmul does.  The gate runs one 4B model on one GPU with a **synthetic** adapter
   (`scripts/make-test-lora.py`, deterministic, no published LoRA required) and requires: the no-LoRA fused text
   `==` the `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1` text (the oracle is exact here); the LoRA fused text `==` the LoRA
   unfused text (the fix's claim); the LoRA arm `!=` the no-LoRA arm (NON-VACUITY — an unapplied adapter would
   make the second check trivially true); with `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_TRACE=1` at least one
   `FUSE_TRACE MUL_Q8_1 DECLINE ne=… readers=… want=…` line on the LoRA arm and **none** on the same arm without
   an adapter (the fusion is not disabled generally, only for the unsafe shape); and a second `-ub 512`, ~2k-token
   arm that repeats the pair so a 512-token prefill graph is built with an adapter loaded.  Negative-tested: the
   pre-fix build fails the pair, the census and the prefill arm.  Required for any change to a fusion's reader
   count or out-node set (`ggml_cuda_find_mul_q8_1_matmul`, `ggml_cuda_should_fuse_mul_q8_1`,
   `ggml_cuda_count_uses`) and every release that touches block 10's dispatcher.
2. **MTP** — the adaptive-MTP four-axis gate, `-n 3000` (2000 floor), `--reasoning on` for R and `off` for
   P/C/K, plus the stock-relative `llama-batched-bench -npl 1,4,8` arm.  Protocol:
   [`benchmarks/mtp-adaptive-methodology.md`](benchmarks/mtp-adaptive-methodology.md).
3. **The UMA GTT expert store** — `scripts/gate-uma-experts.sh` (block 15): same-seed
   greedy text byte-identical across the arena, placement, kill-switch, lock-clamp, cold-cache, fault, no-host-buffer
   and prefill arms; the arena census non-vacuous (`tables > 0`, `evictions > 0`, **0 arena releases**); both shape
   directions (a budget below the set -> arena, a budget that covers it -> placement); the `-lme`-absent **refusal**
   on a model that cannot fit; the prefill **read bytes below "fetch every used expert"**; P4e's recorded answer
   (no fill worker -- no demand fill ever waits >= 1 ms); and (r344) the `mapwarn` / `bufferedwarn` arms: the pinned-mapping warning must
   fire for `--load-mode mmap` and must not for `buffered`, and each arm must take the transport its load mode names.  It is a prefill-path change, so **`gate-prefill-logits.sh`
   is mandatory alongside it**, and a discrete box owes `scripts/gate-host-pool.sh` (the tier must be inert there:
   zero GTT census lines with the envs forced on).  Every arm freezes the heat-map prior
   (`MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`).  Pass `--kld` to run the KLD gate inside it.
4. **Prefill logits** — `scripts/gate-prefill-logits.sh`: mean KLD <= 0.005 and same-top-p >= 98 % against
   a recorded known-good base.  **One base per ROCm line, and the base's file name carries the line**
   (`...-rocm-10.2.0-gfx120X.kld`, 2026-11-22): a cross-line compare is refused, so the maintainer's 10.1 binary
   and the delivery build (`~/bin/build-llama-rocm`, 10.2) each compare against their own reference and
   `fork-refresh.sh --gates` resolves the delivery line's automatically.  **Required for any change to a prefill kernel** (GDN/SSM chunked, FA,
   MMQ/MMVF/MMB, MoE prefill) or to a default-on approximate path.  Protocol:
   [`benchmarks/prefill-logit-methodology.md`](benchmarks/prefill-logit-methodology.md).
   **An all-reduce arm is a prefill-numerics change too** (block 01): it is only reachable on >= 2 devices
   with a `tensor` split, so pass `--sm tensor --device 0,1,2` (r165; a single device has no comm layer and
   the default `layer` split needs no all-reduce, so the bare gate passes a swapped arm as if nothing
   moved).  **A change whose reach is a rank COUNT needs that count's arm too** (r272): the copy-engine
   2-rank form is unreachable at 3 devices, so its gate is `--device 0,2 --sm tensor` with the base
   recorded from the previous form, and the 3-device arm is the proof that the untouched path did not move.
   The base meta records `device=` and `sm=` and the gate refuses a mismatch -- a 1-device base is not a
   reference for a 2-device `tensor` arm -- and since **r285** (`TODO` #323) the base FILE NAME carries them too,
   so the two points a release needs (the single-device `layer` reference and the multi-device `tensor` arm,
   `--dev0-1-2-sm tensor.kld`) **coexist on disk** instead of overwriting each other.  A base recorded before
   r285 keeps its untagged (canonical-point) name and is still found; a non-canonical point is tagged.  The base
   arm is the previous arm via `--ab "GGML_CUDA_ALLREDUCE=hybrid"`.
   **The base is PER TOOLCHAIN, and the build the gate runs must link the line it was recorded on** (r339): the
   base meta records `rocm=`, and the gate refuses a cross-line compare because the low bits of every prefill
   kernel move between ROCm lines.  **The canonical line is 10.2.0** (the one `~/bin/build-llama-rocm` links and
   the one `release.json`'s arms are measured on), and the recorded base carries it in its FILE name
   (`…-rocm-10.2.0-gfx120X.kld`), so a 10.1 build is no longer part of the reference: **10.1 was retired
   2026-11-23** (`INVARIANTS.md` r359-wip: 10.1's hipBLASLt 1.4 is the pre-`c50ea60130f` library, and forcing its
   lib dir into `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` under a 10.2 build made cross-process text comparisons a coin flip).  A base
   recorded on another line is refused by the gate; re-record with `--record` from the previous release's build
   on 10.2 when the reference must move.
   The default arm is an **f16 KV cache**, which skips the quantized-cache prefill paths (the F16 FA staging
   arena, `GGML_CUDA_FA_KV_NATIVE`'s tile read); a change to those also needs `--kv q8_0` (its own base file,
   r156).
   **A change to a decode-band fusion owes two more arms (r201, `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §81):** the same
   `--ab "<fusion kill switch>=off"` at `--ub 4` (a verify width) as well as at `--ub 1`, and
   `test-logits-width-probe P=4096` on the arms.  `--ub 1` alone is **vacuous** where another single-token
   fusion owns the same nodes (the SSM pre-scan fusion does exactly that on qwen3.5/3.6/3.8), which is how
   the gate/beta softplus rounding survived r183..r200.  Quote both arms.  **Judge the fused arm against
   `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1` (the chain), never against the default** (r202, issue #263): a fusion whose
   kill switch covers only part of the band (`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_VERIFY` gates the verify band only)
   is width-uniform and wrong in the default arm, and only the chain shows it.
   A **kernel-family switch** (a new tile/WMMA head cap, e.g. block 05's RDNA3_0 head-512 rows) uses the
   script's `--family-switch` mode, which judges the same absolute limits **at a point inside the gate's
   calibrated domain** and always prints a same-kernel reordering control measured on the spot:
   `--record --ab "<feature-off env>" --family-switch --model <head-256/512 model> --corpus
   prompts/fa-family-switch-gemma4-26B-A4B.txt --ctx 2048 --chunks 10`, then the same command without
   `--record` (`--candidate-env` selects the arm, so a single switch can be judged against a fully-tile
   base).  The absolute limits stay the criterion; `--allow-control-pass` is an explicit opt-in for a
   switch that provably cannot meet them, so an insensitive point cannot pass a bad change.  The mode refuses a point whose base PPL is above `--family-max-ppl` (default 10): at a
   point the model is not confident about, *every* reordering measures far outside the limits (a benign
   `-ub 512` vs `-ub 256` same-kernel change measures 0.105 / 85.0 %, so such a point cannot decide the
   question — `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §46, `archive/work/fa-rdna3-head512-purity/RESOLUTION.md`).
   The `FA_DBG_NMSE` op-level accuracy reference (`test-backend-ops` error magnitude vs the CPU backend at
   the model's own prefill shape) is the second, deterministic leg of a family-switch judgement.
   **A change that reaches the streamed HOST-EXPERT prefill tier owes the host-expert point as well** (r310,
   `TODO` #344 retired): the 27B reference is dense, so it never builds the pool/in-place/staged prefill
   transport at all.  The point is
   `--model /llm/models/Qwen3.8/Flash-Next/GSQ-IQ3_XXS/Qwen3.8-Flash-Next-GSQ-RCO-IQ3_XXS-00001-of-00002.gguf
   --device 0 --pin "MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load,ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0" --extra "-ncmoe 47 --load-mode dio
   --lazy-mode mlockr -ot per_layer_token_embd.*=CPU"` (base PPL 2.8553, self-KLD `0.000000`; ~35 s per pass,
   peak RSS ~71 GiB, so run it alone on the box).  `--pin` is new at r310: the env a point was measured under is
   part of the point — it is folded into the base file name, recorded in the base meta and **refused on
   mismatch** — because on that shape `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT` alone moves the greedy text
   (`benchmarks/2026-11-18-host-expert-prefill-oracle.md`).
5. **The whole set builds** — ONE build of the applied tip in the `all` profile (`~/bin/build-llama-rocm`:
   shipped binaries + every test/example/tool TU; measured 6-10 min cold, ~10 s warm).  **There is no per-block
   build requirement (r332):** a block is a layer on the blocks before it, the delivered artifact is the applied
   set, and the r2 rule "every block commit builds the `all` target on its own" was retired by the maintainer's
   decision of 2026-11-21 (`WORKLOG.md` 261121.011) after it cost 35-55 min per re-base and weeks of wall time
   for a guarantee the artifact does not need — a block that is uncompilable *in the set* fails this build, which
   is the one a consumer compiles.  `scripts/gate-per-block-build.sh` survives as a **bisection tool** for
   localising which block broke a build (`--changed-from <previous release tip>` builds only the moved blocks
   plus the tip); nothing in a release or a per-push flow calls it.  The class it was credited with catching — a
   file a re-base silently dropped while every surviving file compiled — is caught by the file-set reconciliation
   (`TODO` #358), never by a build.  **`test-backend-ops -o <op>` is a CLAIM CHECK, not a delivery gate, for an opt-in change** (r327): the
   full `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` arm is 7 381 cases and ~25 min, which is right when the change's own claim is "the op
   family still passes" (a contributor PR's evidence, or a default-on kernel change), and waste when the change
   is an opt-in scheduler path whose default path is unchanged -- there, the arms that matter are the census
   non-vacuity and the text identity against the previous build.  **The UI fetch is gone (r328):** the delivery now builds the
   embedded UI **off** wherever it does not ship one (`-DLLAMA_USE_PREBUILT_UI=OFF -DLLAMA_BUILD_UI=OFF` in
   this gate, in `scripts/scratch-tree.sh` and in `~/bin/build-llama-rocm`), so `ui-assets.cmake` fetches
   nothing from HuggingFace and runs no npm -- it emits `UI: embedded 0 assets` and a 1.8 KB
   `libllama-ui.a`.  There is no asset cache to seed and nothing to pin: before r328 the asset cache lived in
   the BUILD dir, so every rung refetched the bundle *inside its compile time* and a timeout failed the rung
   (measured r326: rung 14, `UI: download dist.tar.gz.sha256 ... Timeout was reached`, 13 finished rungs
   discarded).  `scripts/ui-dist-cache.sh` and the `HF_UI_VERSION=b12387` pin are retired with it (`TODO`
   #342, `WORKLOG.md` 261120.006).  The container build is unaffected: its `web` stage supplies the assets as
   `ui-assets.cmake`'s Priority 1.  **Run it against the long-lived checkout** (its default
   `--repo`, or `scripts/scratch-tree.sh gate`): ccache keys on the source path, so a per-arm worktree makes
   the first block a full cold build (measured: 5m56s / 85 CPU-min at a new path vs 8.6s for the same
   content at the established one).  The gate prints each commit's build time and the run's total; a first
   block that takes minutes on an otherwise unchanged tree means the path was new.
6. **GPU faults** — `scripts/check-gpu-faults.sh` on the run's journal + log (never grep a log by hand).
7. **The mixed K/V cache (OPTIONAL block `0101`, NOT a default release gate)** — `scripts/gate-mixed-kv.sh`
   runs on the **optional-applied build** (`apply-all.sh --include-optional-all`, then build that checkout):
   `test-backend-ops -o FLASH_ATTN_EXT` in the default, `GGML_CUDA_FA_KV_MIXED=0` and `GGML_CUDA_FA_KV_NATIVE=0`
   arms (a clean N/N, no FAIL/NaN -- the count moves when upstream adds op cases: 7021 at r287, 7453 at r329);
   W=1..8 width purity **maxdiff 0** for q8_0/q8_0 and the three mixed pairs at the requested split; a real
   load + greedy decode per pair; and the non-256-head and qwen4exp refusals (a mixed pair that loads on those
   is a FAIL).  **The two refusal arms always run** since r330: their models are defaulted (Ling-3.0-tiny and
   Qwen3.8-Flash-Next GSQ-RCO-IQ3_XXS on this box, overridable with `--other-model`/`--qsa-model`), a missing
   file is a FAIL, and `--no-refusals` is the only opt-out -- it counts the arms as SKIPPED and prints
   `PASS (INCOMPLETE)`, so a run cannot report `N/N` with an arm it did not exercise.
   It is a new kernel family, so a change to the tile `type_V`, `ggml_cuda_fattn_tile_type_V()`, the mixed
   instance list or the band predicate re-runs it, and the artifact must be re-cut onto the current default
   tree (`optional/mixed-kv/README.md`).  `validate-set.sh` check 8 is what keeps that artifact applicable;
   the delivery's own gates do not cover a mixed pair.
8. **Idle-slot parking** — `scripts/gate-park-idle-slots.sh`: drives a park/unpark request sequence
   (a long prompt on slot 0, a short one on slot 1, a return to slot 0, a launch on slot 2) and requires the
   parking arm to be **byte-identical** to `LLAMA_SERVER_LAZY_IDLE_SLOTS=0`, with the moves traced and no
   refusal.  Required for any change to the parking path: the KV-cache park API, a memory type that carries
   a second cache, or the server's park scheduling.  **Gates 1-3 never reach parking** — it is inert in
   `llama-cli`/`llama-bench`/`llama-perplexity`, so a server-only defect is invisible to them (issue #227
   shipped in r136 and survived r136-r145 on this fact).
9. **qwen4exp hyper-connection fusions** — `scripts/gate-hc-fusions.sh MODEL.gguf`: every arm **pins all
   four switches** (`LLAMA_FUSED_HC_MIX`, `LLAMA_FUSED_HC_COMBINE`, `LLAMA_FUSED_DSV4_HC_PRE`,
   `LLAMA_FUSED_DSV4_HC_POST`; the table is checked at startup), so each hard arm differs from the reference
   by exactly **one** switch and must be **byte-identical to the true unfused chain (all four off)** — that is
   `comb_op`, `both_ops` (graded against `mix_op`, so the combine stays hard with the mixer on), `cuda_fuse_off`
   and, on a chain-mixer class, `mix_op`.  Pinning is the r309 fix (`TODO` #341, `INVARIANTS.md` r309): with
   the DSV4 switches left at their defaults the reference arm was *not* the chain, because
   `fused_dsv4_hc_post` defaults to `meta_split_active` and the DSV4 ops are mutually exclusive with the
   mix/combine ops — so a `-sm tensor` run compared DSV4_HC_POST against HC_COMBINE and read 446 vs 459 on
   Flash-Next IQ4_NL.  With them isolated the IQ4_NL class is **5/5 hard arms identical to the chain** at
   `-sm tensor` (true chain, `mix_op`, `comb_op`, `both_ops`, `cuda_fuse_off` all 450 `bc46095cf4c6`).
   **Numeric arms, sha-anchored per (hc-down class, split state)**: the DSV4 HC pre/post pair (r236, `TODO`
   #292 — they own the HC16 bf16 producer slots, so `LLAMA_FUSED_DSV4_HC_PRE=0` swaps the producer precision as
   well as the fusion; 3 devices `-sm layer`, wikitext 512 x 40, base PPL 2.8489: pre **0.013217 / 96.392 %**,
   post **0.013198 / 96.186 %** against their own chain, and 0.015568 / 96.451 % for the pre op re-measured
   at 20 chunks), the two **shipping states** (`all_ops` on a tensor split, `ops_no_post` otherwise), and
   — r309 — `mix_op` on a **fused-mixer** class, because its inject half is the fused band dot where the
   chain's is the lora GEMM (**mean KLD 0.000000 / same-top-p 100.000 %** vs the chain, 20 x 512 wikitext, but
   a greedy near-tie can flip on a tensor split: that class reads 449 `d62340fbf566` through the chain and 449
   `6a102dbc62d6` through the mixer).  An arm that moves off its anchor **FAILs**, which is the regression
   signal a "reported" arm used to swallow; the `defaults` arm (no env at all) must equal one of the two
   explicit states and prints which, so a flip in the split-dependent default is a FAIL too.  (Historical,
   for scale only: the IQ4_NL class read 0.027936 / 96.471 % at base PPL 1.7888 in the gate's own r156 record
   and 0.021542 / 95.814 % in gate 11's r148 row — different points, neither is a target.  The r148 row's
   "BF16-hc 449 chars `6a102dbc62d6`" is the *chain* of that date; the true chain now reads `d62340fbf566`
   under `-sm tensor`, which is what #292 mistook for drift.)
   Required for any change to `build_hc_mix`, `build_hc_combine`,
   `GGML_OP_HC_MIX` / `GGML_OP_HC_COMBINE` (kernel, CPU reference or op definition) or a matcher that fuses
   either.  **Run it once per hc-down weight class** — Q8_0/BF16 `hc_attn_down` takes the fused mixer, any
   other quant takes the chain (`--types MODEL.gguf` prints the class) — **and once per GGUF layout**, since r303
   a **merged** `hc_*_down_inject` file is a different graph shape (the class line names it; build the fixture
   with `scripts/make-merged-hc-gguf.py`, and pair it with the `test-llama-archs -a qwen4exp` "merged HC" arm,
   whose negative control is a SIGSEGV on the pre-r303 tree).  **A Q8_0-hc file is its own arm of this gate since
   r313**: `blk.0.hc_attn_down` = Q8_0 takes the Q8_0 mixer (a BF16 file takes `hc_mix_bf16_*`), and on a file
   the built-in `fusedmixer` anchors were not recorded from, pass `SOFT_EXPECT=""` so the numeric arms are
   *reported* rather than failed -- the HARD arms (`comb_op`, `cuda_fuse_off`, and `both_ops` graded against
   `mix_op`) and the `defaults`-matches-a-named-state check still apply, and `both_ops == mix_op` is what proves
   the Q8_0 combine+norm fusion (`hc_mix_combine_rms_gamma_q8`) is exact.  Measured at r313 on `UD-Q4_K_XL`
   (Q8_0 hc + F32 inject, 2 devices `-sm tensor`, `--n-cpu-moe 42 -lme auto`): PASS, all ten arms reading exactly
   the r312 build's hash in the same arm (431 `df42686290aa` / 433 `8cd639da2fbf` / 461 `8b39a8568222`).  The gate pins `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0`,
   `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`, `LLAMA_HC_BLK16=0`, `LLAMA_HC_RES16=0` and `GGML_CUDA_MMB_DOWN16=0` itself and its
   default arm must never run through `env -` (GNU `env -i`, which wipes `HIP_VISIBLE_DEVICES` on a pinned
   multi-device run — r303, `INVARIANTS.md`).  The defect class it
   exists for only
   shows on the arms where the two halves disagree, and `test-backend-ops` cannot see it: it compares each
   op against a CPU reference that can share the op's mistake.  Issue #228 was invisible to gates 1-6 for
   the same reason (a wrong combine feeds every residual stream the same value, so text stays coherent on
   the arms that happen to be self-consistent), and it silently degraded every non-Q8_0/BF16 qwen4exp quant
   for ten releases.  The r303 report that the IQ4_NL class "does not read byte-identical across the combine
   arms" was a **gate** defect, closed at r309 (`TODO` #341): it was DSV4_HC_POST vs HC_COMBINE, not the
   combine op vs its chain.

10. **Batched state save / restore** — `scripts/gate-state-pipe.sh`: drives the server's **in-memory prompt
   cache** (`--cache-ram`), i.e. the path a real chat turn pays for, in four arms (`LLAMA_STATE_PIPE_GROUPS=8`,
   `=1`, `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC_READ=0`, and `LLAMA_STATE_ASYNC=0`), and requires (a) every arm's restored greedy
   text to be byte-identical to the **synchronous** arm cycle by cycle, (b) the arm that differs in one
   direction only to reproduce the other (`read1` isolates the save bytes, `pipe` the restore), (c) the run to
   have actually restored (`prompt_n` <= 32), (d) no abort and no GPU fault, and (e) the batched save+load
   (`prompt cache update took`) within `SAVE_SLOW_X` (3x) of the synchronous arm's -- r286's HARD 4, added
   because a region-backed buffer's per-row 2-D state copies made every batched arm **114x** slower than the
   synchronous one on a windowed split (4 686 ms vs 41 ms) while the text stayed byte-identical.  Required for
   any change to `llama_io_write_host` / `llama_io_read_host`, `state_stage`, the state pipelining or its events,
   and **with `KV_WINDOW=<cells>` set** for anything that touches a region-backed buffer (r286, issue #313:
   the window's 2-D directions are 1-D only, in both the buffer interface and the async one).  **No
   other gate reaches the host path**: `llama-cli`/`llama-bench` never save a state, and the file-based slot
   save/restore (`POST /slots/N?action=save\|restore`) goes through `llama_io_write_file` / `read_file`, which
   call `ggml_backend_tensor_get`/`set` per tensor and never touch the stage.  A torn state is a silent wrong
   answer, not a crash, which is why the reference is the synchronous path and not "the text looks fine".
   The **device** class (`llama_io_write_device` / `llama_io_read_device`,
   `LLAMA_STATE_SEQ_FLAGS_ON_DEVICE`) is a different path and this gate does **not** reach it: it is covered
   by `tests/test-llama-context`, whose `cp_d` restores a sequence over the device io path and compares the
   replayed logits and `cp_d_s` compares the restored KV blob byte for byte (`-sm tensor`, 2 GPU).  Run that
   binary for any change to those two classes or to `state_stage`/`llama_state_copy_staged` (r157).

11. **Speculative verify-batch alignment** — `scripts/gate-spec-align.sh`: drives a **two-slot** server
   (`--parallel 2 --kv-unified`) with two prompts of different acceptance, so the adaptive controller holds two
   different draft depths, and requires (a) the concurrent pair to recover at least 10 % with `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN`
   on versus off, (b) the equal-length arm (two identical prompts) not to regress, (c) every **single-slot**
   request's greedy text to be byte-identical on/off, (d) `n_seq == 1` `llama-cli` text byte-identical, and
   (e) the control arm to have actually split decode steps (read from the recurrent memory's `RSPREP` trace)
   while the fixed arm splits none — of the **draft-length** kind, and with the **single-row** kind counted
   separately and bounded (`TAIL_MAX`, default 2): a slot that cannot draft at all (its last token, or a full
   context) presents one row and that step still splits, deliberately (`TODO.md` #111, analysed and declined —
   that second ubatch carries the neighbour's genuine draft rows, so the pass count is equivalent), and the
   count is what makes a regression visible — a change that *manufactures* single-row ubatches fails the gate
   instead of being invisible as it was while they were excluded, and (f) a **mixed-drafter** arm
   (`--spec-type ngram-mod,draft-dflash` with a
   DFlash2 draft, the reporter's own case) to stay within 2 % of its knob-off self with identical draft counts,
   because r153's generic `min` cut shipped a -6 % regression on exactly that chain.  It runs at 2 **and** 3 GPUs
   plus a host-expert (`-ncmoe`) arm, because the penalty scales with the number of devices the duplicated pass
   spans.  Required for any change to
   `common_speculative_draft()`, a drafter's per-sequence depth, the server's step/draft scheduling, or the
   hybrid memory's ubatch split.  **No other
   gate reaches this code**: the coherence, prefill-logit and four-axis MTP protocols all run ONE sequence,
   where there is nothing to align.  The two-slot pair's text is reported, not gated: any fix here changes some
   slot's verify width, so the two arms are two configs — and per-config determinism is a **single-slot**
   statement (2026-10-28: one unchanged binary lands in two text basins on the two-slot pair at 3 GPUs), so judge
   a change by the split count, the single-slot text identity and the pair's aggregate t/s, never by a two-slot
   text diff (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §63).  **Per-box note (r329):** the bound is (a)'s 10 %, calibrated on the
   multi-device and host-expert shapes where the duplicated pass costs 3-54 %; on `fingon`'s single 24.5 GiB card
   the pair is memory-bound instead (pair aggregate 100.5 t/s against 228.3 for the same pair at equal length, and
   the *inert* equal-length arm itself moves +12 % run to run), so that box runs the arm with **`RECOVER=1.05`**
   and records the number rather than gating a threshold three times its own noise.  The strict parts do not
   move: the split count (`TAIL_MAX`, `NCMOE_SPLIT_MAX`), the single-slot text identity and the no-regression
   bound (`REGRESS`).  The host-expert arm's residual 2 splits are **prefill/decode boundary** steps, not an
   unequal cap (`INVARIANTS.md` r329, `TODO` #356 retired).

12. **Graph replay census** — `GGML_CUDA_GRAPH_TRACE=1` on one decode arm, and read the exit line:
   a healthy decode is `direct` ~= 0, `replay` ~= the call count and `properties_changed` in the tens; a
   large `direct` **with a matching `properties_changed`** means the replay identity is churning and
   `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` will measure the same speed (which is how you tell the two apart).  `capture`
   is the number of **distinct keys** (one graph per layer per width: hundreds), so a large `capture` with a
   near-zero `properties_changed` is normal and is *not* churn.  Required for any change to the graph cache,
   its key, the property comparison or anything that hands a captured graph a raw device pointer (the expert
   cache's table buffers).  Then compare `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GRAPHS=1` against the default: once replay works
   it is the **control**, not an equal (r149: 37.3 vs 42.8 t/s on the Flash-Next `-ncmoe` arm,
   `benchmarks/2026-10-26-graph-replay-keys.md`).

13. **Context-checkpoint reuse** — `scripts/gate-ckpt-reuse.sh`: drives a hybrid/recurrent server (a long
   prompt, then three that resume from a cached prefix) in the two `LLAMA_SERVER_CKPT_REUSE` arms and requires
   (a) every request's greedy text to be byte-identical, (b) the keep arm to have kept a restored checkpoint
   and never superseded one while the eager arm did the opposite, (c) the keep arm to create fewer checkpoints,
   (d) no abort and no GPU fault, and it **SKIPs** (rather than passing vacuously) when the config makes no
   usable checkpoints or when the keep path is unreachable (a dense/SWA target restores at `pos_max`, one token
   before the checkpoint's `n_tokens`).  Required for any change to the server's context-checkpoint path
   (`create_checkpoint()`, the restore selection, `common_prompt_checkpoint`) or to the state save/restore it
   uses.  **No other gate reaches it**: the coherence, prefill-logit and MTP protocols never create or restore
   a checkpoint, and the state gates drive the prompt cache instead.  A wrong keep is a wrong answer on the
   *next* resume, not a crash (r155, PR #236).

14. **Speculative-checkpoint location** — `scripts/gate-spec-ckpt-device.sh`: on a hybrid/recurrent target the
   server's *speculative* checkpoint (saved before a verify whose draft is longer than `n_rs_seq`, restored on a
   partial acceptance) now lives in device memory when one GPU has room (`LLAMA_SERVER_SPEC_CKPT_DEVICE`, r184).
   The device arm is **not a byte image** (the metadata is in `data_tgt`, the payload in the context's per-seq
   staging buffer), so a restore whose save it does not match is a *silent* wrong answer.  Three arms on one
   binary (host `=0`, device, `-nkvo`) and the gate requires: the device arm to take the device path and to
   really create checkpoints, the kill-switch arm never to, the `-nkvo` arm to take the host path with its own
   reason (`the context's state is not offloaded`), every request's greedy text plus the drafted and accepted
   counts to be identical between the host and device arms, and no abort or GPU fault.  The `-nkvo` arm is
   excluded from the text comparison on purpose (moving the KV cache off the GPU selects different kernels).
   It **SKIPs** when the device path is not reachable (a multi-GPU split, the state too large for state plus
   512 MiB, or no checkpoint taken).  Required for any change to `spec_ckpt*`, `use_ckpt_tgt`'s
   `update_tgt`/`load_tgt` or the state io classes' use there.  **No other gate reaches this code**:
   `tests/test-llama-context`'s `cp_d` covers the io class, not the server's use of it, and `gate-ckpt-reuse.sh`
   covers a different object (r184, PR #254).

15. **Speculative verify width** — `scripts/gate-spec-width-band.sh` (r185): the aligned draft depth is bounded
   by a verify **width** so the batch stays inside one kernel family.  On a host-expert config that width is the
   expert-cache band (unchanged); on a band-0 config it is `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN_WIDTH_BAND` (default 16), because
   r176 made `band` answer only for a model with host-resident experts and a multi-slot `draft-mtp-adaptive`
   draft was therefore **unbounded** there (4 slots at `n_max 7` = ~28-32 rows).  The gate drives a band-0 3-GPU
   server at 1, 2 and 4 concurrent slots on one binary and **fails** unless: the bound really fires on the
   4-slot arm (`aligned draft depth ... -> 3` with `width band 16`, and never on the knob-off arm), the 4-slot
   aggregate is >= `RECOVER` (default 10 %) of the knob-off arm, the 1- and 2-slot arms are **inert** (no bound
   line at all, byte-identical text, within `REGRESS` 3 %), every arm generated its full token budget, and no
   arm aborts with no GPU fault.  **No other gate reaches this code**: the coherence, prefill-logit and
   four-axis MTP protocols run one sequence (16 / 1 - 1 = 15 is above any `n_max`), and `gate-spec-align.sh`
   runs two or fewer drafting sequences on arms whose depth sits at or below 7, where the bound is inert too --
   it is a *four-slot* property, which is why it went unseen until PR #169's numbers were re-measured on r184
   (+14.2 to +31.8 % on three shapes).  Required for any change to `align_draft_caps()`, the band/width inputs
   or `LLAMA_SPEC_ALIGN_WIDTH_BAND`; every release.

16. **GDN direct-conv fold width purity** — `test-logits-width-probe MODEL prompts/prose-rdna-boosts.txt P
   [ubatch]` at **`P=3584` and `P>=4096`** (`P>=4096` is also the floor the RDNA4 band's 64-column block
   needs), on a **dense** GDN model *and* a **MoE** one, in **one single-device arm and the multi-device
   arms** (`-sm tensor` and `-sm layer` with every GPU present).  The conv fusion and the three folds it
   carries (the snapshot tails written by the conv, the conv-state `GET_ROWS` deferred into it, the 1-token
   direct conv) plus the shared-expert gate fold change a multi-token step's **launch order**, never its
   arithmetic — so every other instrument is blind: greedy text is clean, `gate-prefill-logits.sh --ub 1`
   reads `0.000000 / 100.000 %`, and the probe's own *within-run* `width_purity` is `PASS`, while the arm is
   still unstable **between runs**.  That is exactly how PR #177 was held out (35B-A3B at P=3584 gave
   `2b1caf3bd5cfe4e3` x3 and `c2336484ebf6dab2` x2 against a stable stock arm; the class was that model's
   prefill being sensitive to the launch sequence itself, TODO #75, now closed).  **The multi-device arms are
   the load-bearing half since r186**, which removed `gdn_conv_enabled()`'s device-count test after the
   2026-09-23 3x R9700 `-sm tensor` divergence stopped reproducing — that removal is empirical, not a proof,
   so the sharded arm is what keeps it honest.  It **fails** unless, in every arm: the default arm's `W8`
   and row-0 hashes are identical across `>= 3` runs and identical to the **reference path**
   (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_CONV_FUSION=1`, i.e. `CONCAT`+`SSM_CONV`+`CPY` with no direct conv); the fused arm
   **actually fired** — `GGML_CUDA_CONV_DEBUG=1` must log `GDN_CONV_DIRECT ... snap=1` (match the `snap=`
   field: `n_snap=1` also appears with `snap=0`) — and the reference arm must **not** log `GDN_CONV_DIRECT`
   at all.  Either half missing is a **vacuous pass** and is reported as a failure, not a skip.  Run the
   fold-level switches (`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_GDN_CONV_SNAP=1`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_GATHER=0`,
   `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_CONV_DECODE=0`, `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_SHEXP_GATE_SIGMOID=0`) as a second, finer A/B whenever
   the change is *to a fold* rather than to the conv.  `scripts/validate-gpu.sh` step 4c runs the
   single-device and multi-device arms automatically on a 2+ GPU box when the applied tree touches
   `gdn-conv`/`cpy-batch`/`ggml-cuda-try-fuse`.  Reference (`W8`/row-0, gfx1201, P=4096; r186): 27B IQ3_S
   1 GPU `8bb890b8a89303c8`/`9bbb4af6819e78b7`, 3 GPU tensor `c7358e61f003f775`/`c86873f3774ada7d`, 3 GPU
   layer the same as 1 GPU; 35B-A3B Q3_K_M 3 GPU tensor `930668430bf6c432`/`8bc2462726b575a1`, 3 GPU layer
   `b2ba7832a589aa4b`/`98d90306b32cbdc5`; 35B-A3B Q4_K_M 3 GPU tensor `-ncmoe 40`
   `36f526ba4f3b899c`/`6eaf29f6f3c53d4a`.  Required for any change to a GDN conv fold, its matchers, a
   matcher it rides on, or `gdn_conv_enabled()`/`ple_conv_enabled()`; every release.

17. **MTP draft window** — `scripts/gate-spec-draft-window.sh` (r200, issue #264; r246, issue #293): the MTP
   draft context's KV is a sliding window (`--spec-draft-kv-window`, default `0` since r247), and under `--kv-unified` the one draft cache
   holds every sequence, so it must be sized per sequence — `(win + one batch) x n_seq_max` — by
   `llama_spec_draft_kv_cells()`, which is also what the slab's draft reserve and `common`'s "is a window
   active" test call.  The gate drives 2 slots through 6 requests (the first slot fills its window, the second
   slot's own batch is the #264 failure, then both slots are re-prefilled with the other slot's prompt so both
   rings wrap, then re-used from cache) and **fails** unless: every request answers on both slots (the bug is
   an HTTP 500 `decode() failed: failed to process speculative batch` on the second slot), the windowed arm's
   draft cache really is `(win + ub) x n_slots` cells (a window the cache declined makes the text comparison
   vacuous, and the r193-r199 shape is exactly half), and the windowed arm's text is byte-identical to
   `--spec-draft-kv-window 0` on **every** step.  Since **r246** the gate also asserts the `auto` POLICY, startup-only: no
   window and no message at `-c 8192` x 2 slots (nothing to save), a logged **decline** at the reporter's
   `-c 131072` x 2 slots (the shape where the old quarter-of-the-context sizer granted 32768 and cost 8 % of
   decode), and a granted 65536-cell cap at `-c 67584` x 1 slot.  Since **r247** it also pins the DEFAULT
   itself (the regression that shipped: nothing in the ladder could see it), startup-only: `default_off` (no
   knob at `-c 131072` → **no** window line and no decline), `tensor_default_off` (the same under `-sm tensor`,
   the layout the loss was measured in) and `tensor_auto` (an explicit `auto` still grants the cap there, so
   the two default arms are not vacuous).  That gate's ready-wait also had to stop matching a benign `--fit`
   WARN ("failed to fit params … n_gpu_layers already set"), which killed the server ~1 s in and graded an
   empty log -- found while adding the `-sm tensor` arms.  Since **r248** (issue #297) it also runs
   **multimodal arms** (`mtmd_win` / `mtmd_win0`), because the text arms cannot reach the window's other
   failure mode: text advances the position by one per token, so position-based reclaim always frees exactly
   one batch, while an M-RoPE image chunk puts every row on the SAME temporal position and the `win + one
   batch` spare room then runs out mid-chunk (HTTP 500 `failed to process mtmd chunk`).  The arm shrinks the
   draft ubatch (`MTMD_DUB`) so a stock ~300-token test image is wider than the spare room, and it **fails**
   unless the image batch really is wider than that room, the window arm really needed the ring fallback
   (`ring eviction is active`, absent in the no-window arm), neither arm reports `failed to find a memory
   slot` or `drafting is disabled`, the text is byte-identical on both steps (including a prompt-cache
   re-request), and the reserve estimate and the ALLOCATED cache agree on the same cell count (they read the
   target's `-ub` and the draft context's `MTP_DRAFT_N_UBATCH` cap respectively until r248).  The arms run at
   `-lv 4` — the generated text comes back over HTTP, so a threshold can only pollute the log, and without it
   three of these checks would grade an empty log.  **No other gate reaches this code**: the coherence,
   prefill-logit and MTP protocols run one sequence, where the geometry is unchanged (and identical to
   r198's), and the park/ckpt/state gates drive one slot at a time.  Required for any change to the draft
   window's sizing, `llama_spec_draft_kv_cells()`, `find_slot`'s truncating-window ring, `create_memory`'s MTP
   branch or the derived draft reserve; every release that touches block 14.

18. **The windowed KV across a tensor split** — `scripts/gate-kv-window-split.sh` (r204, `TODO` #124): the
   resident window is one VMM region **per child device** of a `-sm tensor` split, every child committing the
   same `W` cells of its own slice, with the grow fanned out to every child and `--fit` sizing it against the
   split's per-device budget.  Three failure modes make a text comparison vacuous or dangerous, and each has an
   arm: a window that never really spills (the map unit quantises the boundary, so a small slice can sit at 0),
   a grow a child does not answer (the CUDA hook returns 0 = "not a region buffer" for a Meta buffer — a silent
   success on unmapped VA that page-faults the GPU on the next batch), a fit that sizes against aggregate
   free VRAM instead of the per-device budget, and an **even** split (which hides the per-tensor boundary bug
   of issue #314 entirely: an uneven ratio gives two tensors of one cache different cell strides, so a shared
   byte boundary short-maps the shorter ones and asks past the region end on the last).  The gate **fails**
   unless: `win3 == ref3` (3 devices, small model), `win2 == ref2` (2 devices, 27B, a 5246-token prompt so the
   boundary slides) and `win2u == ref2u` (the same model on an UNEVEN `-ts 6,4`, whose debug re-run must show
   two distinct cell strides in one region or the arm is an even split in disguise) byte for byte, the
   window is enabled **once** per cache and one region is reserved **per device**, every device really slid its
   window (`FLIP device <i>` in a debug re-run) with no `refusing to flip`, a 1 MiB map unit changes no token
   and slides more often than 4 MiB, `draft-mtp` on the windowed split is byte-identical to the no-window arm
   **and** to plain (with identical draft acceptance), a `-c 1048576` split (64 GiB of f16 KV) runs only because
   of the window, the single-device and `-nkvo` arms are unchanged (the r197 contract), the fit reports a
   WINDOWED cache on the split and keeps the pinned `-c`, `auto` decides, the pinned-RAM ceiling widens the
   window where it can and drops it where it cannot, and `LLAMA_KV_REGION=0` says nothing about a window.
   `scripts/gate-state-pipe.sh KV_WINDOW=<cells>` is the required companion arm (the batched AND synchronous
   state save/restore of a windowed split; that is where the 2-D D2H of a mixed host/VRAM backing failed, and
   where a per-row copy cost 114x -- r286, issue #313).
   **No other gate reaches this code**: the coherence/prefill-logit/MTP protocols run no window, and the
   single-device window arms do not build a Meta buffer type at all.  Required for any change to
   `ggml_backend_meta_kv_region_*`, the region buffer type/vmm region, `llama_kv_cache`'s region selection,
   the split branch of `common/fit.cpp` or `llama_model::kv_cache_split_mirrored`; every release that touches
   block 14.

19. **KV residency** — `scripts/gate-kv-residency.sh` (r206, the archive plan's phase **P0**,
   `wip/kv-residency-cleanup/` §2.1): for the **window** and the **attendance pool**, a resident arm and a
   deliberately under-sized arm, on one binary.  A residency defect is a *wrong answer*, not a crash, so the
   oracle is same-seed text (plus the drafter's acceptance) -- and that is only an oracle if the arm provably
   LEFT the resident placement, which is why the gate fails when the two arms report the same residency.  **Every
   arm pins the expert-cache state (`MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`, r284)** next to `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0`: the
   persisted heat prior orders residency, residency selects which MoE path serves an op, and the paths are not
   bit-identical on a 2-device split with a drafter loaded -- so an arm that inherits the prior its predecessor
   wrote compares cache states, not placements (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §89).  Window
   arms (4B, `-c 16384` f16, 5 246-token prompt): `wref` (no knob), `wres` (`W=8192`, above the run depth:
   **must** never flip a mapping nor map a host byte) and `wspill` (`W=2048`: **must** do both, `FLIP device 0`
   > 0 and `of which host` > 0), text byte-identical on all three.  Pool arms (Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS + its MTP
   sidecar, 1 GPU, `-ncmoe 48`, q8_0, `-c 32768`, 25 763-token prefix, MTP depth 4): `pref` (no pool, the
   SPARSE arm the pool replaces), `pres` (`--kv-attendance-pool on`, **system-sized**: one decode band =
   4 104 pages against a 6 441-page working set) and `plegacy` (`--kv-region-pool 32768`, the r246 spelling:
   accepted and **ignored**, with `LLAMA_KV_POOL_VERIFY=1`); text and `draft_n`/`draft_n_accepted` identical
   across all three, `bad 0`, claims/resolves/hits non-zero, the derived pool **smaller** than the cache (so
   the claim pressure is real), `plegacy` reporting the compatibility WARN and holding the SAME page count as
   `pres` (the size is not a knob since r247), and a per-id hit rate inside (0, 100).
   `REUSE=1 KEEPOUT=<dir>` re-grades a run; it also prints a per-cache **KV accounting** block from the
   log (cells, bytes, the buffer the placement got, host-backed MiB, pool geometry, the counters line).
   **r363 (GitHub PR #369): the pool is also driven across TWO REQUESTS**, because every other arm is ONE
   `llama-cli` request and nothing in them re-prefills over a resident page -- which is how a 225 M-word
   stale-read defect shipped green.  `pstale_ref` (no pool + `LLAMA_QSA_DENSE_DECODE_UNTIL=0`) is the text
   the pool must reproduce; `pstale_res` (`--kv-attendance-pool on`) must read `bad 0` and equal it byte for
   byte on the re-prefilled request; `pstale_ctl` (the same pool + `LLAMA_KV_POOL_DROP=0`) must read `bad >
   0`, or the section cannot see the class it was added for and a green `pstale_res` means nothing.  The
   prompts are ~18.1k then ~4.1k tokens and share a PREFIX by construction (`PSTALE_ENTRIES`, one seeded
   generator), both sent with `cache_prompt: false`; the arm refuses a shape short of 2 048 re-prefilled
   tokens.  It needs `llama-server` beside `LLAMA_CLI` and adds ~30 s per arm.
   **r217: the pool section also asserts the PROMPT RATE** -- `pres` must keep `PREFILL_MIN_PCT` (default
   40 %) of `pref`'s `prompt eval` tokens/s, both read from the arms' own logs, so a placement that is
   *correct and slow* now fails: block 14's pool was measured at 427.5 against 829.3 t/s at pp32K (-48 %,
   `WORKLOG.md` 261105.001) while every residency assertion passed, because its page-fill path runs per
   DECODE step and a prompt chunk reuses nothing.  The floor sits BELOW that known cost on purpose (a
   regression floor, not a target) and `pfloor` is not a timing arm (it carries the verify oracle).  The
   window arms' prompt rates are printed and **not** asserted: a window below the run depth is a documented
   trade-off whose cost is linear in the depth past the window (`ENVIRONMENT.md` 17.0).  The canonical pool arms need ~72 GiB of host RAM
   (`-lm none -lzm off` = a resident PLE + a pinned host expert set), so on a smaller box pass `MODEL_POOL`/
   `DRAFT_POOL`/`PROMPT_POOL` or grade `--window-only`.
   **The counters that cannot be asserted are in the script header**: `hits %` can never read 100 % (a block
   claimed by the same launch is neither hit nor miss) and a resident pool does not reach ~0 misses (the claim
   cursor is round-robin and the top-k selection moves) -- at `-c 32768`, depth 25 763 and 8 192 pages,
   144 claims/resolve is the resident arm's floor, and the floor-vs-resident delta is what the gate compares.
   **Negative-tested**: an off-by-one pool slot in `kv_pool_map_fill_kernel` (a wrong-page resolve) diverges the
   text and the acceptance, and the gate FAILs.  **r264: on a shared host/device pool the pool is REFUSED BY
   DESIGN** (`TODO` #300 retired), so that platform grades the refusal instead of the placement: exactly **one**
   `--kv-attendance-pool was declined` WARN, no arm engaging the pool, and the KV unchanged against a no-pool
   `pdecl` arm on the **same** (default) decode arm -- `pref` is the *sparse* arm the pool replaces, so it is not
   that reference.  Two `--fit` placement-hint arms cover the advice a failing fit prints: `hintwin` (a trunk with
   no sparse indexer: `--kv-window` must be offered, `--kv-attendance-pool` must not) and `hintpool` (the pool
   model: **absent** on a shared pool, **present** on a discrete GPU, so an absence-only check cannot pass).  **Multi-device pool arms (r213)** run the same three arms on
   `MULTI_DEVICES` (default `0,1`, `-sm layer`), judged **within** the device count (the reference is the 2-device
   no-pool arm `pmref`, never `pref`): `pmres`/`pmfloor` must be byte-identical to it, the enable line must report
   the device span, and the teardown counters must report **one counter block per device** -- that last assertion is
   the one that catches the r213 defect (a shared counter block or a device-invariant mirror buft name makes the
   resolve node unclaimable and the *load* aborts).  `MULTI_DEVICES=0` skips the section on a 1-GPU box.  Required
   for any change to the window/region provider, the pool, `is_streaming()`, the sparse decode arm, block 14's
   `--fit` interaction, `memory_breakdown()`'s KV accounting or anything that makes a buffer type per device;
   every release that touches block 14.  **r222 adds the provider's own SELFTEST as a gated arm pair**
   (`wselftest` must PASS and `wselftest0`, with `LLAMA_KV_REGION_QUARANTINE=0`, must FAIL), so the primitive's
   oracle is proven sensitive on every run instead of on trust -- it was a documented-but-ungated diagnostic
   whose verdict was an INFO line, i.e. invisible at every default until r222.  ~15 s (`--window-only`),
   ~5 min (`--pool-only`), ~4 min (`--multi-only`), ~10 min (all).

20. **A Windows-only change (r209, PR #269)** — there is no Windows toolchain on the primary box (`TODO` #95),
   so the gate is **inertness on Linux**, and it is proved rather than inspected: compile each changed
   translation unit with the delivery's own command (`build-rocm/compile_commands.json`) as `-E -P` on the
   previous and the folded tree and diff the token streams — identical apart from `__FILE__`/`__LINE__`
   strings is the claim "the Linux build compiles the same code".  A header delta wholly inside
   `#if defined(_WIN32)` is inert by construction; a new `.cu` must be checked to compile to an empty TU off
   Windows (and it needs a cmake re-run: `ggml/src/ggml-cuda/CMakeLists.txt` globs `*.cu` without
   `CONFIGURE_DEPENDS`).  Then the ordinary tip gates as evidence rather than as a search: coherence on 1 GPU
   and a 3-GPU `-sm tensor` arm (byte-identical to the previous release), `gate-prefill-logits.sh`,
   the whole-set build after the fold, `check-gpu-faults.sh`,
   and `validate-set.sh`, whose portability lint scans the added file (tier 1).  **The Windows arms are the
   reporter's numbers and say so in the block notes** — this gate cannot validate them, and a release that
   claims otherwise is wrong.  r209: 4 TUs token-identical, coherence `897 a77fa8c1107a` / `964 eab86065e67e`
   (== r208), KLD 0.000000 / 100.000 %, per-block 4/4, no faults.

21. **Host-expert pool** — `scripts/gate-host-pool.sh` (r219, issue #274; thirteen arms since r277 — eleven since r273, seven since r224; **r299: the `pinlate` (mixture) arm is gated on the LOGITS, not the text** — the script records its own KLD base from the `ref` config and requires mean KLD <= 0.005 / same-top-p >= 98 % for `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN_FAULT=late`, plus a non-vacuous mixture (pools built before the latch, pre-latch fills, rolled-back fills); the arm's `late` latch also waits for the first routed op now, because sizing-at-registration had left it with 1 pool of 141, and the WHOLESALE arms keep the byte-identical text check (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §95); the r277 pair is the PLACEMENT READ QUEUE's: `qd1` (`MOE_EXPERT_FILL_QD=1`) and `bulkbuf` (`MOE_HOST_POOL_PLACEMENT_MODE=buffered`, renamed from `MOE_HOST_POOL_BULK_DIO` in r294), with census assertions that the queue really ran and that the placement read is O_DIRECT (the text is identical with the queue inert, so text alone cannot gate it); the teardown-census  **r262:** a change to the HOST-READ TRANSPORT (`--load-mode buffered|dio|none`, the adaptive `auto`, or `pool_transport_*`; `MOE_HOST_POOL_TRANSPORT`/`_DIO` were removed in r294) also owes `scripts/gate-host-read-transport.sh` (every arm must take the transport it names, proven by its own counters; `auto` within 10 % of the best pinned arm on a 1000-token decode; `MOE_HOST_POOL_STAGE_SPAN=0` must REFUSE the buffered arm) and, because it is a default-on path, the MTP four-axis + `batched-bench -npl 1,4,8` pair.
   arm since r227; the latched-pinned-tier arm since r229; the pinned-tier kill-switch arm since r233; the two `-sm tensor` split arms -- `tsplit` and `tsplitnosink` -- since r273, asserting the guard's rule rather than one geometry's answer since **r295** (`TODO` #334: a retirement must be PAID FOR by a landed demote, `0 demoted` must be explained by a geometry decline or the new `sink no-demote tables` census line, and full inertness is required exactly when the census says no pool can express a demote), and that SINK=1 changes no byte there): one pool-backed host-expert config
   (Flash-Next, `--n-cpu-moe 47`, `--load-mode mmap`, a `MOE_HOST_LIMIT_FRAC` low enough that the set does not
   fit), arms and same-seed greedy text: shipped (`--load-mode mmap`) / `--load-mode dio` (r294: the load mode IS the demand
   transport, and it builds a pool over the file-backed master) / `MOE_HOST_POOL_DIO_FAULT=1` on that arm (a
   platform with NO pool disk tier: the Windows shape) / `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_FAULT=1` (no source can be
   resolved at all) / `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_FAULT=late` (each table gets one source, then loses it: the mid-run
   shape of the issue #274 follow-up) / `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK=1` (the eviction demote: an admitted miss copies its
   arena victim into a free pool slot, and the arm must actually demote or it is vacuous) /
   `MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK=1 MOE_HOST_POOL_SINK_FAULT=1` (every demote copy fails, so the arm proves the #274 rule
   holds for the demote too: **`0 demoted`** with `copy-error` declines > 0 -- a failed demote declines and is
   never published as a resident victim, which would serve stale bytes for the life of the run) /
   `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN_FAULT=late` (r229, issue #278: the platform **latches the pinned tier off** after the pools
   and the arena exist -- the Windows shape at the adapter's shared-segment ceiling -- and the latch must stop the
   tier GROWING, not throw away pinned memory the run already owns: the census must report
   `N expert fills were served from a host pool built BEFORE the refusal` with N > 0, measured 7 060 against 0
   with the pre-r229 lookup order, which also turns 110 failed fills into 1 587).  Every arm runs
   with `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`, so the arms are compared across one prior.  It **fails** unless the three healthy arms are byte-identical with a
   real pool (slots > 0, MiB pinned > 0) and no fill failure, the disk-tier arms are not vacuous, and the two
   failure arms are still byte-identical with `FAILED fills: N`, `the slot is NOT resident` per table and
   **`takeover=0`** (the call that fails a fill must decline; taking the op over leaves `input_cpy` unfilled
   while `moe_cache_get_table` refuses the same table — the text alone can hide that behind a lucky stale
   copy), and that the census prints **once, from the model teardown** (`host pool (L2)` above
   `model teardown: dropped ...`): `moe_cache_report` is an `atexit` hook inside ggml-hip.dll, so on Windows it
   runs after `main` and llama-server's async log is gone (r227, the reporter's clean Ctrl+C printed every
   teardown line except the census).  Two rules it exists for: a fill must never require a tier the platform may
   not have (the mapping is always a source), and a fill that fails must roll the admission back and decline the
   op rather than serve the slot.  Required for any change to a pool fill/staging path, `expert_host_src()`, the
   cache's admission, the `-lme`/`--host-experts pool` placement or the loader's host-expert source registration;
   every release.  ~3 min on one GPU (the pool prewarm dominates).

22. **Narrow-row norm fusion aliasing** — `scripts/gate-norm-rows-alias.sh` (r225, issue #276): the
   `RMS_NORM -> MUL(w)` fusion (and its sigmoid-gated `-> MUL_MAT -> SIGMOID -> MUL` form) reads `x`, `w`
   and the gate output `z` while it writes `dst`, but the gated form dispatches the gate `MUL_MAT`
   **itself**, ahead of the launch.  In graph order `x` is dead as soon as the `RMS_NORM` has consumed it
   and the gate output has exactly `x`'s shape, so the allocator's best fit for it is `x`'s own block.
   The gate drives the fusion **without a scheduler** (`scripts/norm-rows-alias.cpp` calls
   `ggml_backend_graph_compute()` directly, i.e. none of the alloc deps the sched's optimize pass
   registers exist) and forces each aliasing shape by pointing a tensor at another one's buffer after
   allocation: `alias_gate` (gate output == `x`) and `alias_dst_row` (fused output == `x` + one row) must
   be **declined**, `alias_dst_exact` (fused output == `x` exactly, a read-then-write of the same address
   by the same thread) must still **fuse**, and every arm must reproduce `ref` byte for byte and equal
   the driver's own `--no-fuse` (chain) digest.  It then drives the **delivery path** on a real gated
   GGUF (Ling-3.0-tiny, `-c 8192` over the 5256-token `prompts/prose-rdna-boosts.txt`) and fails unless
   the gated form **fused** (>= 1 `norm-rows: ... fused=1 gated=1` line, so the text comparison cannot be
   vacuous), **nothing was declined** (the scheduler's alloc deps still hold `x`/`w` for the fused
   launch: this is the assertion that catches a dropped or renumbered dep, which is silent -- the text
   stays correct because the guard falls back), the fused and `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_NORM_ROWS=1` arms are
   byte-identical, and neither arm aborts or faults a GPU.  `GGML_CUDA_NORM_ROWS_DEBUG=1` is the
   diagnostic that makes the decision observable.  A **third** arm (r238, GitHub issue #285) drives a
   **SILU**-gated hybrid (Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0) instead: the gated matcher records the gate `MUL_MAT` and then
   declines there (the `UNARY` is not `SIGMOID`), so it is the only arm that exercises "a declined gated
   match followed by the plain match", and it fails unless the PLAIN form fused, **nothing was declined**
   and the text equals the kill-switch arm.  A sigmoid-gated model cannot stand in for it (its gated match
   succeeds, which is why the Ling arm passed before and after that fix).  Required for any change to
   `norm-gated.cu`, to the norm-rows alloc-dep registration in `ggml-cuda.cu`, or to the fusion's dispatch
   order; every release (no other gate reaches this code, and `test-backend-ops` cannot: the fused kernel
   is compared against a chain, not against an allocator).

23. **gfx1201 grid-stall pad** — `scripts/gate-grid-stall-pad.sh` (r226, issue #277, extending #273): the
   pad is DEFAULT ON and is a *launch-shape* change at six sites, so two properties have to be observed
   rather than assumed: a padded launch must not change a byte (a wrong row guard or grid-stride bound
   computes a row twice and can still read plausible on the shapes that do not pad), and the pad must
   actually FIRE at the site it claims to cover.  **A1** runs the `GET_ROWS` op oracle in both arms on
   four shapes added for this (block 08: vec float `(1,1,256)`, plain float `(1,4,64)`, the sub-block
   dequantizing path and the super-block k-quant path, the last two `(1,4,64)`) so every one of the four
   `get_rows` launches is dispatched at a pad-able 256-block grid, and requires the CUDA backend to match
   the **CPU** reference in both arms plus a `get_rows_*` pad line for each site in the pad arm and none
   with `GGML_CUDA_GRID_STALL_PAD=0`.  **A2** runs `MUL_MAT_ID` (a MoE expert-cache remap gathers with
   `grid.x = 16384`, so one extra y row would add 16384 blocks and stay a multiple) and requires the
   refusal branch to be reached: the helper must DECLINE that pad instead of launching a block row that
   cannot help.  **B** requires same-seed greedy text to be byte-identical between the pad arm and the
   kill-switch arm on a dense 1-device arm, a `-sm tensor` 3-device arm and a MoE `-ncmoe` arm, with a
   verbose 1-token probe of each arm proving the pad fired (pad arm) and never fired (kill-switch arm).
   `GGML_CUDA_GRID_STALL_PAD_DEBUG=1` is the diagnostic all of that rests on.  Required for any change to
   `ggml_cuda_grid_stall_pad()` or a call site of it, and every release (the site that was missing is what
   issue #277 reported: `get_rows` was unpadded from r46 to r225).

24. **The one-command verdict for a re-base (r236)** — `scripts/gate-rebase-release.sh`.  A re-base has to answer
    "did we lose performance, and are the numerics still pure?" in one place; this orchestrates the gates that own
    the rules (build per block, prefill-logit KLD, the `MUL_MAT_ID`/`MUL_MAT` op suites, the coherence texts, the
    host-expert multi-slot DFlash acceptance arms (`scripts/gate-server-arms.sh`, below), the interleaved
    performance A/B against the shipped build, and the GPU-fault scan scoped to the run's own start)
