    and prints a single PASS/FAIL with exit `0`/`1`.  It adds no thresholds of its own beyond `--tolerance`
    (default 1.0 % per `llama-bench` arm) and `--text-tolerance` (default 3.0 % for the real-text prefill arm).
    **It FAILS rather than skips**: a step that cannot run is a FAIL, so a missing build,
    model or recorded hash cannot look like a pass.  **Step 5b (`--only textprefill`) is a real-text prompt-eval
    against the shipped build, and it is not redundant with the `llama-bench` perf arm**: `llama-bench` builds its
    prompt from `std::rand() % n_vocab` (uniformly random token ids), so every ubatch routes over every expert and
    the expert arena is measurably inert there (pp1450 = 414.1 / 414.5 / 414.5 / 414.5 t/s at
    `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB` 0 / 4096 / 8192 / auto) while the same arm on the shipped prose prompt reads
    401.6 / 583.6 / 676.9 / 1063.6 t/s.  A `llama-bench`-only perf step therefore cannot see the arena or the
    host-expert transport, which is where this repo's prefill risk lives (`TODO #273`, `#296`).  `--quick` drops the build ladder and the KLD gate while
    iterating; `--perf-only` is the A/B alone and is also the answer to a `#279`-class "is this still worth
    implementing on the new base?" question (the arm it guards is the decode arm).  Run it before a tag and paste
    its verdict into the release's `WORKLOG.md` entry.

    **Step 5c (`--only server`) delegates to `scripts/gate-server-arms.sh`** (`TODO` #295): three two-slot
    `llama-server` arms with a DFlash drafter on host-resident experts (`-ncmoe` 24 and 10 on Flash-Next GSQ
    + DFlash2, 10 on 35B-A3B Q8_0 + its DFlash2), `-sm layer`, greedy, the shipped prose prompt in both slots.
    It exists because every other step in this ladder — and the MTP four-axis protocol — runs a SINGLE
    sequence, so a draft regression on a `-ncmoe` multi-slot run is silent (acceptance -> ~0 while the text
    stays correct); that is the guard R13 dropped, and this is its detector.  HARD: the server comes up, both
    slots draft, per-slot acceptance is not a collapse (`ACC_FLOOR`, default 0.5 x baseline) and has not drifted
    past `ACC_TOL` (default 0.10), and the two generated texts match the recorded hash set.  **The text is a
    legitimate oracle only because the server runs `--no-kv-unified` with a deterministic admission** (pinned
    `id_slot` plus a warm-up request on a third slot, so both real requests are queued before either is batched);
    `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §38 forbids a concurrent two-slot text hash under a UNIFIED KV cache, which this arm used to
    do — that is what `TODO` #349 was, and §103 is the rule that replaced it (`WORKLOG.md` 261121.012).  `-c` is
    per slot, i.e. three slots means `3 x CTX`.  R13's own numbers
    (0.53793/0.51678 etc.) are **not** the criterion: its invocation was never recorded and they do not
    reproduce, so the baselines are measured at r236 and reproduce to the digit (0.72269/0.74359 on Flash-Next
    at both `-ncmoe` levels, 0.44720/0.59124 on 35B-A3B).

25. **Fusion-window leaf-copy aliasing** — `scripts/gate-fusion-alias-copy.sh` (r239, issue #287):
    `ggml_cuda_check_fusion_memory_ranges()` decides whether a fusion may run from byte-range overlap and used
    to skip every leaf (`src->op == GGML_OP_NONE`) as "a weight".  A scheduler copy of a **host-resident expert
    table** is a leaf in the **compute** buffer, and the allocator's last-use rule is computed on the unfused
    graph, so the fused gate+up+GLU wrote the GLU output into the gate-weight copy it was still reading (NaN
    logits).  The arm needs host-resident experts served from scheduler copies, a ubatch inside the extended
    MMVQ MoE band (9..16 columns) and that ubatch offloaded, so it is `--n-cpu-moe 99 -fa on -ub 14` with the
    expert cache off and `GGML_OP_OFFLOAD_MIN_BATCH=9`.  **A** requires the **fused** arm's text to equal the
    recorded anchor `3fab4da87774` and not to be degenerate, and the fused-vs-chain equivalence is a
    **logit** compare, not a text hash (r340, `TODO` #359): `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence` at the same
    shape (base recorded **in-run** from the `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_FUSION=1` chain, both legs pinned
    `ROCBLAS_USE_HIPBLASLT=0` + `MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load`) must read mean KLD <= 0.001 and same-top-p
    >= 99.9 % (measured 0.000008 / 100.000 %; the same-kernel `-ub 8` reordering control at that shape reads
    0.003746 / 93.701 %, so the limits are not vacuous).  A text hash cannot be the oracle there: the unfused
    chain moved at the r328 re-base (block 09's host-offload relaxation changes the graph's partitioning and
    with it the per-op kernel path) and the two arms are one near-tie apart (194 vs 193 chars).  Plus >= 1
    `FUSE_TRACE ALIAS .../copy@` line with `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_TRACE=1` (without it the arm never reached the shape
    and the comparison is vacuous); the `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_MMVQ_MOE_BAND=1` control must match the chain's
    text too.  **B** repeats it with `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_FAULT=200` (a declined fill is served from the
    scheduler's copy -- the reporter's other way in) and must equal the fused arm's text, i.e. the anchor.
    **C** is the production arm (expert cache ON, same ubatch): it must equal the recorded anchor
    `3fab4da87774`, i.e. the extra declines change no output, and it must log a decline, i.e. the check is not
    inert there.  Negative-tested: the pre-fix build fails A (58 chars `793107d73635`), A's trace, B and C.
    Required for any change to `ggml_cuda_check_fusion_memory_ranges()`, to a fusion's out-node set, or to
    host-expert scheduler copies; every release (no other gate reaches this shape).

26. **An hc base copy is released per statement, and one BF16 src1 block per graph** — `scripts/gate-hc-base-copy.sh`
    (r240, issue #290).  The `hc_combine_norm` [repeat] matcher reads the base from a **pool copy** when `add`
    or `mulg` reuses its buffer; r236 registered that copy on the device `pool_stack` *before* the window's
    `set_bf16*()` pushes, and a LIFO pool only frees a killed block when it reaches the top — so one copy per
    layer stayed live to the end of every graph, i.e. ~2.2 GiB on a 26-layer device under `-sm layer` (the
    reporter's 259.6k-token OOM).  The two per-graph activation caches (BF16 src1, MMB) had the same class of
    retention: they leapfrog, each replacing its block only after the other pushed a newer one, so neither
    chain ever reached the top.  `GGML_CUDA_POOL_STACK_CENSUS=1` is the instrument (a WARN, so the census arms
    pass `-lv 2`): per graph per device it prints `live=… dead=…/…MiB bytes=… | hc=<live>/<MiB> made=…
    mmb=… bf16=…`, where the per-kind counts are LIVE entries and `dead` is the killed-but-blocked pile.
    **A** is two text arms (delivery defaults and `GGML_CUDA_BF16_SRC1_CACHE=0`) that must both equal the
    recorded anchor and each other, so a "fix" that changes numerics fails.  **B** is the census arm on the
    delivery defaults: every device must report `hc=0` **with `made>0`** (a shape that stops reaching the
    matcher also prints 0, which is why the copies-made floor exists), `bf16 <= 1` and `mmb <= MMB_LIVE_MAX`
    (the two caches hold one block and `cache_max` = 4, so more is churn), and the retained bytes under
    `MAX_RETAINED_MIB`.  **C** repeats B with the BF16 cache off: nothing may be cached.  Negative-tested: the
    pre-fix build fails B with `hc=31/620.0` live on device 0, `dead=59/521.8` MiB and 1854.6 MiB retained
    (vs `hc=0`, `dead=1/24.0` and 292.0 MiB); a build without the census fails on "no census lines".
    Required for any change to the hc base-copy path, `bf16_src1_cache_put()`, or the pool stack.
    `INVARIANTS.md` r240, `benchmarks/2026-11-09-issue-290-hc-base-copy.md`.

27. **The node budget vs the graph** -- `scripts/gate-node-budget.sh` (r267, GitHub issue #302): a
    **quantized** KV cache carries the Hadamard rotation, and since upstream #31073 the rotation constants are
    per layer on the device, so every layer adds 12 nodes and 2 leafs that `graph_max_nodes()`'s per-tensor
    heuristic does not model.  A model with few matmuls per layer (phi-4: 40 layers, 243 tensors, fused QKV) then
    needs more entries than the scheduler's table has, and the `split_only` probe path has no size assert, so it
    aborts with a bare `ggml-impl.h:337` instead of saying why.  The gate runs four seeded greedy arms on one
    model (quantized K/V at `-ub 2048` and `-ub 512`, `f16` K/V, and the quantized arm with
    `LLAMA_ATTN_ROT_DISABLE=1`) and requires every arm to create a context, generate text and log **no**
    "over its node budget" line, plus a GPU-fault scan of its own window.  `--rotation-ref` additionally requires
    the quantized arm's text to equal the rotation-off arm's (the rotation is an identity).  **It is the only gate
    that builds a graph tight enough to reach the budget**: coherence and MTP use one KV type on models with a 20x
    margin, `test-backend-ops` never builds a llama graph, and the per-block gate only compiles.  Negative-tested
    2026-11-12 on unpatched `a691b2345`: the two quantized arms abort (exit 134), the `f16` and rotation-off arms
    pass.  Required for any change to `graph_max_nodes()`, the scheduler's sizing, a memory module that adds graph
    nodes/leafs, or anything that changes a graph's node count per layer.  **The tight shape is now on the primary
    box**: `/llm/models/phi-4/IQ4_XS/phi-4-IQ4_XS.gguf` is the gate's `MODEL` default and all four arms pass
    (`31 chars sha=a1b7eb2ee7a6`, r328, 2026-11-21) — before that the gate was owed on every box (`TODO` #354's
    record), and `halo`/`fingon` still lack the file, so a release arm there must say "not runnable, no tight
    shape" rather than "pass".

28. **The expert cache's alias ROLE and its take-over promise** (r298, `TODO` #335/#336 retired) — the consumer
   resolves a routed expert table from the scheduler's `input_cpy` **address**, and the graph allocator recycles those
   addresses: a layer's `ffn_gate_exps`/`ffn_up_exps`/`ffn_down_exps` share a device and a layer number, so an alias
   guarded by device + layer only resolves a *sibling role* and the redirect serves the neighbour's arena in place.
   `moe_cache_repair_copy` covers the other half (a take-over's "I skip the copy" promise broken by an eviction between
   the hook and the consumer's launch).  Verify, on a config whose cache really serves in place (`get_ok > 0` and
   `identity` tables at `-lv 4`):
   * the **role arms** — `MOE_EXPERT_ALIAS_ROLE_CHECK=0` against the default on Flash-Next GSQ-IQ3_XXS + its MTP
     sidecar (2 devices `-sm layer`, `-ncmoe 48`, `-c 32768`, prior absent, 64 tokens): `0` reads one text and the
     default reads the *other*, and the default must equal the staged (`MOE_EXPERT_PREFILL_IN_PLACE=0`) arm.  Pass
     is `837 chars sha=f40ae789f219` (`=0`) against `835 chars sha=7a3474cbe5f1` (default **and** staged) — i.e. the
     gate asserts the two arms AGREE on the default, not merely that each is stable;
   * the guard must be doing the work: `-lv 2` `alias_find_checked: ignoring a stale MoE-cache alias (… role …)` once
     per run (a run that never refuses is a vacuous arm — the refusal is the finding);
   * the **promise repair** — `MOE_EXPERT_TAKEOVER_FAULT=1` on the same model at `-c 8192` on
     `prompts/kld-corpus-35b-in-domain.txt`: the exit census must read `take-over promises broken=<n> repaired=<n>`
     with `n > 0` **and** `repaired == broken`, and its greedy text must be byte-identical to both no-fault arms
     (r298: `595 chars sha=c0e7e91d84c4`) — a repair that is skipped, or that changes the text, is the failure.
     **Since r307 this arm is `scripts/gate-moe-promise.sh`'s `fault` arm (gate 34), which runs it as a control
     next to the arm that keeps a STALE promise from being repaired — a promise is valid for one staging pass
     only, and that half of the contract is the issue-#327 regression, not this one;**
   * **`gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "MOE_EXPERT_PREFILL_IN_PLACE=0"`** on a host-expert arm of the same model
     (`--ctx 2048 --chunks 7` on `prompts/kld-corpus-35b-in-domain.txt`, 2 devices `-sm layer`, `-ncmoe 48`):
     mean KLD **0.000000** / same-top-p **100.000 %**.  This is the measurement that says the in-place read is a
     base-pointer swap, and it is the arm that `TODO` #336 was owed;
   * plus `scripts/gate-host-pool.sh` (the host-expert scheduler path) and `scripts/gate-kv-residency.sh` when the
     change also touches residency.
   * **the placement arm (r321, issue #332)** — any change to `moe_cache_fusion_serve()`'s three-state answer owes a
     **staged** arm, because the tip's own configs usually are not: the `-lv 4` census on a table that is neither
     served in place nor slot-remapped must read `mixed_lanes=0` with the gate-less kinds in the **staged** column
     (`mmvq_glu=<in_place>/<staged>`), where a vacuous "gate lane satisfied" term reads `mixed_lanes>0` and silently
     stands the fusion down.  35B-A3B UD-Q3_K_M, 1 GPU, `-ncmoe 24 -c 8192` is such an arm (`166/0 mixed_lanes=112`
     pre-fix against `166/112 mixed_lanes=0`, text `ba65a38cf871` both); the same arm with
     `MOE_EXPERT_TAKEOVER_FAULT=1` is the both-lanes-staged + broken-promise case (`mixed_lanes=200` against 0,
     `broken=216 repaired=216`).  A flat staged config (`MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=2048`) and the Flash-Next
     `-sm tensor` shape read `mixed_lanes=0` on BOTH builds, i.e. they are **vacuous** here — do not use them.
   A byte validator cannot substitute for any of this: `MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_VALIDATE=3` reads 0 `MISMATCH` on the NaN arm
   (`INVARIANTS.md` r298 fact 3).

29. **A mid-run tier fault's MIXTURE is gated on the LOGITS, not on the text** (r299, `TODO` #282 retired) --
   `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN_FAULT=late` leaves the pools built before the refusal serving and the rest of the tier stood
   down, so one run mixes cache-served and scheduler-served ops.  That mixture takes a different fp32 association
   order (~1e-5; measured mean KLD 0.000001 / max 0.000051 / same-top-p 100.000 %) and flips a near-tie token on some
   layouts, so the generated text is NOT its criterion -- `scripts/gate-host-pool.sh` records a KLD base from its own
   `ref` config and requires the mixture inside **0.005 / 98 %**, with the mixture proven REAL (pools built before
   the latch, pre-latch fills, rolled-back fills, `get_ok`/`decline_all` non-zero).  Required for any change to
   `MOE_HOST_POOL_PIN_FAULT`'s latch condition, to `g_routed_op_seen`, or to the gate's mixture arm; the WHOLESALE
   fault arms keep their byte-identical text assertion and still fail on a divergence.  A text match is not evidence
   about the arithmetic -- `MOE_HOST_POOL_FILL_ORDER=rank` reproduces `ref`'s text on a build that diverges
   (`INVARIANTS.md` r299, `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §95).

30. **The server's prompt admission policy** (r300, block 16 since r301, the contended-only cap and `-ib` at
    r306) — `scripts/gate-prefill-fairness.sh`:
    two arms on one binary (`-np 2`, one GPU), `baseline` = `FAIR=0 CHUNK=0 DUTY=0` (the r299 slot-order fill,
    which is also the *uncontended* window at r306, so the lone-prefill arm below is now a like-for-like
    comparison rather than a 3 % tolerance for the cap) against `candidate` = the fair share + `-ib` + the duty
    cycle.  HARD: **ADMISSION** — the short
    prompt's TTFT `<= (k-1 placement + 1 alignment step) x p x 1.25`, where `k` is its own measured step count
    (from its `return_progress` chunks) and `p` its own measured per-step cost (`prompt_ms/(k-1)`); **FAIRNESS**
    — the TTFT ratio `>= min(3.0, 0.8 x the shape's own ceiling)`, the ceiling being the baseline's wait over
    that floor, so the bound is DERIVED and not a fixed 3x (a streamed host-expert arm reads 2.6x where
    `L/(k*chunk)` promises 2.9x and a fixed 3.0 rejected a correct fix; a pooled shape honestly reads ~1x and
    says why); the worst decoder ITL `<= baseline_max x (chunk/n_batch) x 1.75` (and `x0.75` on a streaming KV,
    where a per-step spill read does not scale with the chunk); a lone long prompt's prefill within 3 % (r306:
    reads -0.4 %, because with nothing decoding both arms take the whole `-b` window -- and that is the arm that
    would catch a regression that re-capped an uncontended step); **one**
    request's greedy text byte-identical **on a fresh server per arm** (a pooled cache carries the earlier
    requests' page residency into the oracle and can flip a near tie — §52/§63); each arm's startup census
    matches the configuration it claims; no abort and `check-gpu-faults.sh` clean with `--since` bounded to the
    run.  `--self-test` runs the candidate AS the baseline and requires the ADMISSION and FAIRNESS checks to
    fail — verified to reject the r299 build (6x its own floor, 1.0x).
31. **The prefill/decode duty cycle** (r301, block 16) — `scripts/gate-prefill-duty.sh`: two arms on one binary
    (`-np 2`, one GPU, both slots pinned), `baseline` `DUTY=0` against `candidate` `DUTY=16` (the default).
    HARD: **the burst is real** — decode tokens delivered while the other slot prefills `>= 3x` (0 is not a
    pass: the metric is the tokens, not a latency median, because the tokens arrive in a burst); **the trade is
    bounded** — the concurrent prefill's own wall `<= 2.5x` the baseline's (the design caps the idle time at one
    prefill step, so the analytic bound is 2x; the slack is the first, still-uncalibrated cycle); a lone prompt
    (nothing decoding) within 3 % and **0** "no tokens to decode" lines, because the deferral must never fire
    into an empty batch; one request's greedy text byte-identical; the census states `duty`; no abort; GPU
    faults clean.  **r306**: the lone-prompt arm is now the two-sided one -- with nothing decoding the step must
    be the WHOLE `-b` window (r301 capped it at the chunk), and with a decoder already generating the prefilling
    slot's per-step admission must stay `<= -ib`; the `-lv 5` `fair share: budget/added/batch` line is the
    instrument (wide steps carry no decode token by construction, so the two assertions cannot both be satisfied
    by a step that admits a decoder's wait).  With `--spec-args` it also requires the gain to hold under a drafter with acceptance not
    collapsed (`>= 0.5x`, judged for collapse only — §63).  Run it on the dense 4B shape, on the 27B +
    `draft-mtp n3` shape, and on a host-expert/streamed shape (`--server-args "--n-cpu-moe 47" --env
    "MOE_EXPERT_CACHE_MIB=8192,MOE_EXPERT_HEATMAP=load"`).

32. **The GDN gate/beta epilogue for F32 and K-quant projections** (r304, block 10, contributor PR #322) --
    `scripts/gate-gdn-epi.sh`: two models whose `ssm_alpha`/`ssm_beta` are the two new projection types (F32:
    Qwen3.6-35B-A3B UD-Q4_K_M; Q4_K: the LMStudio Q4_K_M file -- `MODEL_F32` / `MODEL_Q`).  Both fusions are
    default-on, RDNA-gated and produce the same text whether or not they fire, so the gate is a **mechanism** gate
    first.  HARD: with `GGML_CUDA_GDN_EPI_LOG=1` the ON arm must log the **decode and the verify** line of its own
    family (`gdn_epi: gate_beta decode|verify f32` from the mmvf launch, `gdn_epi: decode|verify <type>` from the
    ksplit epilogue) and must NOT log the other family's (the two matchers are type-disjoint), while the OFF arm
    (`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_EPI=0 GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GATE_BETA_F32=0`) must log no `gdn_epi:` line at all -- that is what
    makes the lines attributable to this feature instead of to another fusion.  HARD: the ON text is byte-identical
    to the OFF text at every ubatch in the sweep (`2, 4, 8, 512`: both sides of the ksplit verify band and the
    `ncols > MMVQ_MAX_BATCH_SIZE` mmq switch above it, where the epilogue must be inert); `-ub 1` is not reachable
    from llama-cli on these models (`-b 1` aborts in `common.cpp`'s `get_sub_batch`, unrelated), and that point is
    `gate-prefill-logits.sh --ub 1` instead.  HARD: no abort and a clean kernel journal.  `--self-test` runs the
    mechanism check against a pre-r304 binary, which must produce no `gdn_epi:` line (it is the negative control,
    and the gate refuses a build that already has the feature).  `tests/test-fusion-gate-beta-multiseq.cpp` is the
    companion unit gate: its F32 and Q4_K arms (77 shapes) need no model, and the F32 arm is negative-tested --
    dropping the F32 branch's `gb_x2 ? 2*ne01 : ne01` row count fails all 11 F32 shapes.  Required for any change to
    `ggml_cuda_gate_beta_bf16_try_fuse`, `mmvq_gdn_epi_find`/`ggml_cuda_gdn_epi_verify_try_fuse`, the ksplit
    body's `gdn_epi` epilogue, the concat reorder's `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_EPI` branch or the fusion's memory rule, and
    every release.  It cannot force the memory rule's **Q8_1-activation half** (no llama.cpp graph carries a Q8_1
    activation into a `MUL_MAT`, so no model arm exists) -- that half rests on review, and the arithmetic on the
    unit test.

33. **A host-expert routed MoE runs on the GPU at EVERY ubatch width** (r305, block 09, GitHub issue #323) --
    `scripts/gate-moe-host-offload.sh`.  The op used to be judged by `GGML_OP_OFFLOAD_MIN_BATCH` (default 32) when
    it was above the cache band (16 on RDNA4), and a `ROCm_Host`/`ROCm_HostMap` weight is owned by no GPU backend
    (`ggml_backend_buft_is_cuda()` is a function-pointer identity on the DEVICE buffer name), so a **17..31-token**
    ubatch ran its MoE on the **CPU** at 31x the cost.  HARD: on a 19-token prompt (`-sm layer`, `-ncmoe 40`, one
    GPU) the shipped default places **zero** `MUL_MAT_ID` nodes on the CPU in the scheduler's own
    `GGML_SCHED_DEBUG=2` assignment dump; `GGML_MOE_HOST_OFFLOAD=0` places **some** (non-vacuity: without it the
    arm cannot reach the window); and the text is byte-identical across the default, the kill switch and
    `GGML_OP_OFFLOAD_MIN_BATCH=9` (the reporter's workaround).  HARD: the CONTROL -- a >= 64-token prompt places
    zero CPU nodes in **both** arms (the generic threshold already offloaded it) with equal text, which is what
    proves the rule closes exactly the 17..31-token window.  Census runs are `-lv 5` + `-n 1`, text runs carry no
    `-lv` at all.  This is the only gate that reaches the width: `gate-host-pool.sh` drives `-ub 1024` and a
    6-token prompt, and `gate-moe-host-offload.sh` is required for any change to
    `ggml_backend_cuda_device_offload_op()`, to `GGML_MOE_HOST_OFFLOAD`, to the delivery's host-expert master
    buffer types, or to the split that decides whether such a weight is GPU-owned.

36. **A speculator must not change the greedy text on a multi-device tensor split** (r319, block 05, GitHub
    issue #330) -- `scripts/gate-spec-purity.sh`.  Manifest A (q8_0 K/V, prose prompt, reasoning off) and B
    (bf16 K/V, reasoning prompt, reasoning on), 27B Q8_0, `-n 512`, seed 42, temp 0, 3 devices `-sm tensor`:
    `--spec-type none` and `--spec-type draft-mtp --spec-draft-n-max 3` must read **one text**, and the draft
    arm's Generation t/s must be >= 1.2x the plain arm's (NON-VACUITY -- a speculator that drafted nothing
    would satisfy the equality trivially).  `--control` runs manifest A with `GGML_CUDA_FA_SPLIT_CONST=0`
    (the pre-fix `ntiles_KV`-driven split) and requires it to **diverge**, so the gate is shown to have teeth.
    It also runs the four-axis protocol's R/P/C/K prompts on the same 3-device tensor split on both caches
    (`--n-axis`, default 512; `--no-four-axis` to skip), requiring plain == draft per axis -- the arm issue #330
    asked the four-axis gate to gain.  And it runs `test-logits-width-probe` at `P=4096` for q8_0 and bf16; the probe's new `boundary` arm
    prefixes at `256*(P/256 + 1) - NW/2`, the prefix where W=1 and W=8 land on different
    `GGML_PAD(used, 256)` extents (a flat prefix on a 256-multiple puts every width in one padding block and
    cannot see this class).  **Since r322 it is also the gate for the band's own fixup period** (`P`,
    `ggml_cuda_fattn_band_wmma_split`, issue #333): `P` is the band's summation order, so a tiles-dependent `P`
    is exactly the "decode and its own verify must reduce identically" class -- the probe's flat and boundary
    arms are the purity proof and per-device `P` differences (32/64/64 on a 3-device 27B split) are legal
    because a device's attention never crosses the split.  Required for any change to the tile FA launcher's KV
    split (`parallel_blocks`, `gridDim.y`, the efficiency search), to `ggml_cuda_fattn_band_*` (including
    `ggml_cuda_fattn_band_wmma_split`), to the KV extent/padding (`get_n_kv`, `LLAMA_KV_N_PAD_*`), or to a
    speculator's verify batch composition -- and every release that touches block 05.

37. **The opt-in `-of tp-ffn-even` even FFN split** (r338, block 09, contributor PR #342) --
    `scripts/gate-ffn-even-split.sh`.  The FFN split granularity is the reduction's grouping, so this gate is
    about the SWITCH, not the rate: **A** the default is inert (no `FFN granularity` line, greedy text
    `c420dfe04c42` at the recorded 2-device Flash-Next point) -- a default that carried the reordering is the
    promotion bug; **B** `-of tp-ffn-even` prints exactly one
    `FFN granularity 128 -> 64 ... splits 320 / device (opt-in: -of tp-ffn-even)` line and the text is the
    recorded opt-in reference `cbdb63954bbb` (and DIFFERS from A, so a tag that did nothing cannot pass); **C**
    the four spellings agree (`-of`, `LLAMA_TP_FFN_EVEN=1`, `LLAMA_ARG_OPTIONAL_FEATURES=tp-ffn-even`,
    `-of "tp-ffn-even,"`); **D** `-of nope` is refused naming the tag and listing `tp-ffn-even`, and `-of list`
    exits 0 with it; **E** the 3-device 512-wide arm (35B-A3B Q8_0) is untouched -- no line in either arm and
    identical text.  `--perf` re-measures `draft-mtp` decode both ways without gating on it (throughput is not a
    correctness signal).  `-lv 2` on the census arms only: a hash arm must run with no `-lv`.  Required for any
    change to `get_split_granularity`'s FFN branch, the `common_optional_features()` table, the `-of` handler,
    or `ffn_even_logged`'s per-model placement.

38. **The file-set reconciliation — every block still carries what the patch it replaces carried** (`TODO` #358,
    `INVARIANTS.md` r331/r341) -- `scripts/check-rung-file-sets.py`, called by `scripts/validate-set.sh`
    (check 9) and by `scripts/validate-gpu.sh` (step 1b).  This is the ONE class no build can see: a re-base rung
    that lands **fewer files than the patch it replaces** compiles fine, because the missing hunks live inside
    other files -- that is how block 00's `ggml/src/ggml-alloc.c` half (+121/-2, `GGML_GLU_INPLACE` + the
    unused-view release + the `layout_fp` fingerprint) was dropped by the r328 re-base and shipped through r328
    and r329 while the block's own text, `ENVIRONMENT.md`, the per-block gate table and `tests/test-alloc.cpp`
    kept describing it (issue #345).  The check compares each block against the same block of the previous
    release on five item kinds -- **file** (with its added-line count), **define** (`#define NAME`), **env**
    (`getenv("NAME")`), **option** (a long option added to `common/arg.cpp`) and **block** -- and exits 1 on a
    LOSS (gone from the whole set), a SHRUNK (a file whose added-line count fell by `--shrink-pct`, default 50 %,
    while the set's total for it did not grow) or a GONE (a block/artifact).  A disappearance that is DELIBERATE
    is a decision row in `patches/FILE-SET-RETIREMENTS.tsv` (`<from-release> <block> <kind> <item> <reason>`),
    scoped to the transition it was decided in; restoring the content is the normal answer otherwise.  **During a
    re-base, run it per rung** -- `--rung NNNN --new-chain ~/llama.cpp --new-base <base>` reads the block from the
    applied chain (a chain is the DEFAULT blocks only; `--new <ref|dir>` compares a whole release) -- and then on
    the whole set before the tag; `--warn-only` reports without failing, for triage.  Non-vacuity is recorded: on
    r325->r328 it reads exactly the r331 incident (LOSS `GGML_GLU_INPLACE`, LOSS
    `GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_ROPE_SET_ROWS`, SHRUNK `ggml/src/ggml-alloc.c` 275 -> 155 added lines, plus the block-12
    -> block-16 server move), and across the 70 consecutive release transitions r263..r339 it fires **only** there
    and on the r287->r288 block re-org (2 blocks renumbered) -- zero findings on the other 68.  Required before any
    tag and after every re-cut patch.

39. **[BETA-ONLY since r352: block 17 is withdrawn to `beta/prefill-decode-arms/`; this gate is NOT a release gate]** **A speculator ON the two-arm server** (r350, block 17, `TODO` #364) --
    `scripts/gate-arms-spec.sh`.  The pair accepts `--spec-type` since r350, which makes it the only shape where a
    drafter reads the target's hidden rows from a SECOND context, where the draft/verify/accept round runs on a
    thread that is not the main loop, and where a verify batch is **co-batched** (every active slot contributes
    its own `n_draft + 1` rows).  The gate is three-way and refuses to be vacuous: **A** `pair + draft-mtp` ==
    `pair + none` == `single-context + draft-mtp` byte for byte, one fresh process per arm and one **cold** prefill
    per request, on 3 devices f16 / 3 devices q8_0 / 2 devices f16 (the `deep` arm adds `single + none` as a
    fourth; the device lists are `DEVS3`/`DEVS2` so a box with one discrete device can run the same checks --
    `-sm tensor` then degenerates to one backend and the Meta `stage_input` path is NOT reached, which the result
    must say), with three distinct prompts so no slot reuses another's cells; **B** the mechanism is really on --
    the log must read `decode ctx n_batch = NP*(n_max+1)` OR the v2 candidate's `decode arm 0x.. with
    NP*(n_max+1)/NP*(n_max+1)` equivalent (a 1-row decode arm means the verify width was never configured),
    `decode arm: thread up` OR `decode arm started (own batch of N rows)` (not a single-context fallback),
    `#acc drafts > 0` with a mean accepted length > 1.2, and no `failed to process the speculative batch` (both
    forms, because the beta artifact's log wording moved and a gate that parses only the WITHDRAWN block 17's form
    fails a working pair -- `INVARIANTS.md` r371-wip); **C** `draft-mtp-adaptive` reads the same
    hashes; **D** the MTP protocol's four axes on the pair (R with reasoning on, P/C/K off) read one text per axis
    with acceptance not collapsed; **E** a concurrency arm (NP requests in flight) completes with content, drafts,
    and shows no abort/assert -- **stress only, not a text oracle** (§63: a co-batched shape is not one);
    `check-gpu-faults.sh` over every log.  **Every arm pins `LLAMA_SERVER_PARK_IDLE_SLOTS=0`**: the co-owner guard
    declines parking on the pair while the single-context server parks by default, and the two placements read
    different texts on a near-tie (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §107, `INVARIANTS.md` r350 item 5).  Required for any change
    to the decode arm's batch composition or width, `common_speculative_add_target_ctx()`, the MTP per-batch
    target-context read, the draft ingest's `arms_quiet` bracket, `spec_slot_reset()`, the speculative round in
    `decode_arm_loop`/`accept_draft_round`, or the arms+draft reserve sum (`common_draft_reserve_derive`).

40. **A shipped shared library must RESOLVE its symbols, and a guard change owes the other configuration's
    build** (r351, issues #353/#354) -- `scripts/check-shlib-symbols.sh`.  r347-r350 shipped a `libggml-hip.so`
    carrying `U ggml_cuda_pool_peer_copy_ok`: the DEFINITION sat inside `#if defined(GGML_USE_VMM)`, its CALLER
    (`ggml_cuda_peer_copy_ok()` in ggml-cuda-slab.cu, a backend-shared file) did not, and a `GGML_HIP_NO_VMM=ON`
    build -- the HIP **default**, so every stock packager build -- linked the library and then failed every
    executable that linked it (`ld.bfd: ... undefined reference to ggml_cuda_pool_peer_copy_ok`).  Two facts kept
    the ladder blind and are why this is an instrument, not a habit: a **shared library is allowed to carry
    undefined symbols** (the failure lands in the consumer's link, and `cmake --build` reported the library target
    as built -- the stale executables were simply not relinked), and **which linker refuses differs** -- GNU
    ld.bfd checks a shlib's undefined symbols by default, lld (what ROCm's clang drives on this box) does not, so
    the same no-VMM build links clean here and fails on the reporter's `x86_64-linux-gnu-ld.bfd`.  The check runs
    `ldd -r` -- the loader's own relocation report, linker-independent -- over every shipped library in
    `<build>/bin` and FAILS on any `undefined symbol:` or `not found`.  Negative-tested on the tree before the fix
    (it names the symbol on the no-VMM build, whose default-VMM sibling is clean) and green on every library of
    both builds after it.  A **guard** change (`#if defined(GGML_USE_VMM)`, a backend/config `#if`) still owes a
    build of the OTHER configuration: a caller and its definition both behind the same `#if` compile out
    together, and neither this check nor any single build can see that.  Required after the release's `all`
    build, and for any change to a conditional-compilation guard, a function a header declares for every
    configuration, or a header-shared API.

41. **The GDN output norm folds into a SILU gate too** (r353, block 10, contributor PR #357) --
    `scripts/gate-gdn-norm-gate.sh`.  Block 10's fold computes a GDN layer's output `RMS_NORM * w` inside the
    `gate -> Q8_1` kernel and retires its `RMS_NORM` + `MUL` launches; r102 matched a SIGMOID gate only, i.e.
    qwen4exp, while qwen35 / qwen35moe / qwen3next / clover-moe emit `ggml_silu` -- so on the delivery's own GDN
    families the fold was dead code (48 GDN layers on the 27B running two extra kernels per step).  It is a
    MECHANISM gate first, because a fold that stops matching changes no hash: with
    `GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_NORM_GATE_CENSUS=1` the ON arm must log `fused silu > 0` with `fused sigmoid = 0` at the
    decode width (`-ub 1`, driven with `llama-perplexity` -- `llama-cli -b 1` aborts in `get_sub_batch`,
    pre-existing) AND in the verify band (`-ub 8`), and the OFF arm (`GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_NORM_GATE_SILU=0`) must
    log `declined silu > 0`; the two counts are NOT gated on equality (a two-slot unified server reads 384 vs
    672).  Then the ON text must be byte-identical to the OFF text at `-ub 2, 4, 8, 512` (the folded norm
    reduces like `rms_norm_f32`'s strided path, so the oracle is identity).  `--kld` adds the exact-fallback
    logit arms a release owes -- `gate-prefill-logits.sh --ab "GGML_CUDA_FUSE_GDN_NORM_GATE_SILU=0"` at `--ub 1`
    and `--ub 4`, 0.000000 / 100.000 % (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §60 rule 2) -- and `--self-test` proves the mechanism
    check has teeth (a pre-r353 binary logs no census and must fail it; a build that already has it is refused).
    The `xn->ne[3] != 1` guard is untouched, so no arm reaches a multi-sequence ubatch (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §49;
    `tests/test-fusion-gate-beta-multiseq` 77/77); Windows / RDNA3 / NVIDIA remain untested here.  Required for
    every release and for any change to the matcher, the skip, the kernel or a GDN output-gate shape.

42. **A "bit-identical to the CPU" GPU kernel matches the CPU's rounding, not its source** (r356, block 17,
    contributor PR #358) -- `scripts/gate-tq.sh`.  Block 17 reproduces `ggml_vec_dot_tq{1,2}_0_q8_K` exactly,
    which is the block's whole design, and the one detail it depends on is invisible to every text/hash arm:
    ggml-cpu's `_mm256_add_ps(_mm256_mul_ps(..), sumf)` is **not** contracted (a Release build emits
    `vmulps` + `vaddps`, no `vfmadd`), so the kernels' per-Q8_K-block accumulation must be an unfused mul and
    add.  The submitted patch used `fmaf`; the claim "every NMSE exactly 0" did not reproduce (**25 of 61**
    `MUL_MAT` cases read ~1e-14, and the split is the diagnosis: every `k=256` case 0, every `k>256` case
    not).  Arms, all HARD: (1) `test-backend-ops -o MUL_MAT -p type_a=tq` 61/61 and `-o GET_ROWS -p type=tq`
    16/16 **with `LLAMA_TEST_OP_NMSE=1` reading exactly 0 on every reported value** (305 and 84 here), with
    the fused-accumulation failure named from its `k=256`-vs-`k>256` split; (2) the three-arm text identity --
    the default, `GGML_CUDA_TQ_FUSE=0` and `GGML_CUDA_TQ=0` (the CPU backend) must read ONE text on TQ2_0 and
    TQ1_0, at a 64-token and a 200-token generation, on 1 GPU and on the multi-device split; (3) the census
    (`GGML_CUDA_TQ_CENSUS=1`): the default arm must show `small>0 vec>0 vec_fused>0` (and `mma>0` on RDNA4),
    the fuse-off arm `vec_fused=0`, and `GGML_CUDA_TQ=0` **all five counters 0** -- a kill switch that leaves
    the path live is not a switch, and a fusion that never fires is a vacuous arm; (4) default-path neutrality
    -- a non-TQ model's greedy text == the recorded reference, so block 17's new fusor and node reorder are
    inert for the models the delivery validates; (5) `check-gpu-faults.sh` clean.  `--self-test` points arm 1
    at a pre-fix binary, which must FAIL.  Required for every release and for any change to `tq.cu`, the
    fusor, the `graph_optimize` reorder or the `supports_op` predicate.

43. **The server's prompt tokens are not the template's to forge** (r357, block 12 amendment, contributor PR #360)
    -- `scripts/gate-tool-text.sh`.  A tool result, a tool call's arguments and an assistant's own text are DATA,
    and the server rendered the template and tokenized it with `parse_special`, so a `<|im_end|>` in that data
    became the real token and forged a turn.  Three parts of the claim fail **silently** -- no abort, no moved
    hash, a fluent answer -- so none of them can be checked by looking at a reply:
    (1) **the mechanism** (census `LLAMA_SERVER_TOOL_TEXT_CENSUS=1`): a forged turn in a tool result, in an
    earlier call's arguments and in an earlier assistant reply must each read `mode=text strings>0` and a
    special-token count **equal to the same conversation without the forged text**, and a clean request must read
    `mode=none`; the forged characters must still be in the prompt as text (the request did not change, only what
    became a token);
    (2) **the kill switch is not vacuous**: the same probes with `LLAMA_SERVER_TOOL_TEXT=0` read `mode=off` and a
    **higher** special-token count -- a switch that leaves the mechanism live, or a census of zeros either way,
    fails;
    (3) **the text the model reads**: `/apply-template` on every probe must be **byte-identical** to the
    switch-off arm.  The default arm's prompt is a token array detokenized back for that endpoint, so this is the
    end-to-end form of `tool_text_tokenize_ok()` -- the piecewise re-tokenization re-derives
    `llama_vocab::tokenize`'s BOS/EOS and per-fragment behaviour, and a vocab whose pre-tokenizer adds a prefix or
    normalizes a fragment (a `metaspace_prepend_scheme "first"`, a SOPRANO / NEMOTRON_3_5_EMBD / MMBERT pre-type,
    a `<mask>` LSTRIP) would move a character with nothing to see in a reply;
    (4) **the fallback is per REQUEST**: on a server started **with an mmproj**, a TEXT-ONLY forged request must
    still read `mode=text` and its prompt must equal that server's own switch-off arm.  The submitted patch keyed
    the fallback on the mmproj *capability* and inserted a space into the tool data on a text-only request
    ("< /tool_response>"); a `mode=space` there is a FAIL;
    (5) **a request that really carries media** takes the fallback and is still protected (`mode=space*`, the
    template's own count);
    (6) **default-path neutrality**: a request with no protected string reads `mode=none` and its prompt token
    count and greedy text are identical to the switch-off arm;
    (7) **the strip is armed with the feature**: a client's own U+FDD0/U+FDD1 is dropped by the default arm and
    KEPT by the switch-off arm, so the kill switch really restores the previous request;
    (8) `check-gpu-faults.sh` clean, no arm aborted.
    Non-vacuity is proved on the build, not in a comment: on the submitted (pre-fix) build the script FAILS (4)
    and (7) -- 29 passed / 3 failed there against 33/33 on the delivery build.  Required for every release (it is
    cheap and it is the only arm that reads the census) and for any change to the protection, the marks, the
    fallback predicate, the mtmd routing in `handle_completions_impl` / `handle_count_tokens`, or the chat-parse
    path in `server-common.cpp`.

44. **`--fit` must not reserve a staging arena the kernels never fill** (r360, blocks 09+10, GitHub issue #367)
    -- `scripts/gate-fit-staging.sh`.  The FA prefill staging arena is a raw device allocation outside the
    compute-graph reserve, so `llama_get_memory_breakdown()`'s no_alloc (`--fit`) path charges a per-device
    compute reserve for it.  That reserve used to be a FLAT `2 * GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB` (1024 MiB at the
    512 MiB default) for every device except RDNA3_5, i.e. for **every** `-fa on` context with a quantized K
    cache -- including the delivery default `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`, where the kernels read the cache natively
    and the arena never grows.  The cost is not visible on a roomy card and is not a rate on a tight one: at an
    ~11 GiB budget a 27B UD-IQ3_S reads a compute tier of 1539 MiB and `-ngl 55` with the flat reserve,
    515 MiB and `-ngl 62` without it (r359: 1539 / `-ngl 52` on the GSQ cut), against a same-tree upstream
    build's 541 MiB / `-ngl 59` -- which on the reporter's 12 GiB gfx1031 is 4.0 t/s against upstream's 15.5.
    The bound now asks the launcher's OWN predicate (`ggml_cuda_fattn_kv_pair_native_host`, i.e.
    `ggml_cuda_fattn_tile_type` on a probe tensor -- the `need_f16_K/V` test of
    `ggml_cuda_flash_attn_ext_get_f16_extra_data`), so the reserve and the launcher cannot drift (TODO 71).
    The gate is `llama-fit-params` only (no compute, ~10 s), four arms: **A** a native q8_0 pair must project
    within 8 MiB of an f16-KV control and must never offload FEWER layers than that control (a q8_0 cache is
    the smaller one); **B** `GGML_CUDA_FA_KV_NATIVE=0` -- the arm that really converts -- must still reserve
    >= 900 MiB over A and must lose layers, which is the non-vacuity proof (an unconditional 0 passes A);
    **C** `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB=0` on the staged arm must raise the reserve to the whole-F16-KV estimate,
    so the accounting still follows the cap; **D** a live `llama-cli` run's own compute buffer must stay below
    the fit's projection (the reserve is an estimate of an allocation the compute buffer does not contain, so
    a run that already spent it would mean a double count).  NEGATIVE-TESTED: on the r359 build the same
    script FAILS A1 (+1024 MiB), A2 (`-ngl 55` < 59) and B1 (0 MiB over the native arm).  Required for any
    change to the staging arena, to `ggml_cuda_fattn_tile_type` / a `_kv_*_supported` predicate, to
    `GGML_CUDA_FA_STAGE_MAX_MB`, or to `llama_context::memory_breakdown()`'s reserve accounting.

45. **An exact sampler fast path must be checked against the path it replaces, not against itself** (r361, block 12,
    GitHub issue #366) -- `scripts/gate-fast-topk-penalties.sh`.  The S2 fast path hands the chain only the `k` tokens
    top-k will keep; since r361 it also reproduces a **penalties** sampler (the affected set is the logit-bias set
    plus the penalty's last-n window, at most 256 distinct tokens, each scored with the sampler's own arithmetic in
    the chain's order) instead of standing down for one, which is worth **4-6 %** of MTP decode on any profile that
    sets a penalty.  Its failure mode is a slow text drift, so no arm may be read alone:
    (A) **the ORACLE is the full-vocabulary arm**: `GGML_LF_FAST_TOPK=0` disables the fast path, which IS the r360
    behaviour for a penalised profile, so the default arm's text must equal it byte for byte on a 3-device
    `-sm tensor` MTP shape and a single-device shape, for presence 0.15, repeat 1.15, frequency 0.3, all three, a
    penalty combined with a logit bias, and no penalty at all.  The arms must be LIVE: the penalised arm's text must
    differ from the no-penalty arm's, and the bias+penalty arm's from the penalty-only arm's (else the pair proves
    nothing about the combination);
    (B) **the CENSUS is the non-vacuity check** (`GGML_LF_FAST_TOPK_CENSUS=1`, a separate `-lv 4` run -- a `-lv 4`
    stream is never hashed): the penalised arm must read `samples fast > 0`, the same count `with a penalty
    window`, and `full = 0`; the `GGML_LF_FAST_TOPK=0` arm the mirror image; the no-penalty arm `fast > 0` with an
    EMPTY window.  Identical text proves nothing if both arms fell back;
    (C) **the SELF-CHECK and its control**: `GGML_LF_FAST_TOPK_SELFCHECK=1` scores the whole vocabulary the way the
    chain would, per sample, and must read 0 mismatches; `=2` swaps the fast path's two best-ranked tokens, so it
    must report mismatches on every sample AND move the text -- that is what makes (A)'s "identical" sensitive to a
    single rank swap instead of inert;
    (D) **the cap falls back, exactly**: a 700-token run at `--top-k 60 --repeat-last-n 8192` must grow the window
    past 256 distinct tokens, so the census must show `full > 0` and the run's text must still equal the
    full-vocabulary arm's (a switch mid-run moves no token);
    (E) `check-gpu-faults.sh` clean.  Required for every release and for any change to
    `common_sampler_fast_topk_k()`, `set_logits_topk()`, the affected-set/penalty arithmetic, the two
    `llama_sampler_penalties_get_*()` accessors, or the census/self-check.

46. **A request that cannot fit beside the running slots must be deferred, not launched** (r365, block 16, contributor
    PR #370) -- `scripts/gate-kv-admit.sh`.  With a unified KV cache (the **default** server: `-np -1` resolves to 4
    slots and `kv_unified = true`) the server launched every request that found a free slot, so two prompts whose sum
    exceeds the context both prefilled until the cache was full and then **decode** failed: the retry loop can only
    clear *idle* slots, so it halved `n_batch` to 1 and answered HTTP 500 "Context size has been exceeded." (with a
    speculator, "speculative batch index N is not inside the current sub-batch").  `server_slot_sched::admit` is now
    asked once per task, before a candidate slot is chosen; a refusal defers through the existing "no slot is
    available" path.  **No other gate can see this class**: every other gate drives one request at a time, and a
    concurrent-server TEXT comparison is forbidden as an oracle (`GREEDY-PURITY.md` §38) except in the one shape the
    gate uses.  The arms, each on a fresh server (`-c 8192 -np 4 --kv-unified`, a 4B Qwen3.5 GDN hybrid so the
    accounting meets a recurrent+attention cache, ~4 min):
    **fit**: two prompts that fit side by side are both answered and **not one deferral** occurs, with each text equal
    to the `LLAMA_SERVER_KV_ADMIT=0` arm's (the amendment moves nothing where it is not needed);
    **over**: two copies of `prompts/prose-rdna-boosts.txt` (5246 tokens, stateless) are both answered, at least one
    deferral fires, and the second request's wall time is greater than the first's;
    **purity**: each `over` answer is **byte-identical to that same prompt run alone on a fresh server** -- the
    deferred request re-prefills all 5246 tokens and must land on the same text;
    **over0**: the same pair on the kill switch answers **500 twice** (NON-VACUITY: `--self-test` runs every arm with
    `LLAMA_SERVER_KV_ADMIT=0` and requires the gate to FAIL -- measured: 12 failing checks, so the gate can reject the
    pre-amendment server);
    **drain**: four overlapping prompts are all answered, serialised, with >= 3 deferrals;
    **reserve**: the cells charged over the prompt read `n_ctx / n_parallel` (2048 at `-c 8192`/4 slots, 4096 at
    `-c 16384`/4 slots), `256` with `LLAMA_SERVER_KV_ADMIT_RESERVE=256`, and the request's `n_predict` when it is
    smaller -- the reserve census is the only place the formula is observable;
    **nouni**: `-np 4` without `--kv-unified` defers nothing and answers both (a per-slot cache cannot have slots take
    each other's cells);
    **shut**: SIGTERM with a deferred queue pending exits on its own (a task that was never launched is answered by
    `server_context::terminate()`; without that the exit hangs inside HIP's teardown -- measured, and the defect was
    pre-existing, only made ordinary by this feature);
    **spin**: a task that waits is offered ONCE and the log does not storm over a window in which nothing can
    legitimately change (the wake re-offers only when no slot is processing, which is what makes it provably
    spin-free), and the server idles at ~0 CPU ticks once the queue drains;
    plus no `GGML_ASSERT`/abort in any arm's log and `check-gpu-faults.sh` clean.  Required for any change to
    `server_slot_sched::admit`/`select`, `kv_admit_ok`/`kv_admit_cells`, `LLAMA_SERVER_KV_ADMIT[_RESERVE]`, the
    wake logic in `update_slots`, or `server_queue::take_deferred_tasks`.
47. **An optional artifact's tree must BUILD, not only apply** (r367, `optional/mixed-kv` `0101`, GitHub issue #371) --
    `scripts/gate-optional-build.sh`.  `validate-set.sh` check 8 proves every `optional/*/01xx-*.patch` still applies
    strictly and produces `release.json.optional`/`.optional_tree`, but **nothing compiled an optional-applied tree**:
    the r360 re-cut rebased `0101` onto block 10's new `ggml_cuda_fattn_kv_pair_native_host` and kept both functions
    while dropping the outer one's closing brace, so `fattn-rdna.cuh` (97 open / 96 close) put every later function
    definition inside a function body.  The patch applied, the tree re-verified, every gate stayed green, and the tree
    did not compile from r360 to r366 (`function definition is not allowed here` at `fattn-rdna.cuh:369` onwards,
    first hit by the reporter on Windows/ROCm 10.2 with gfx1100 in the target set).  The gate applies the default set
    at `release.json.base`, applies every artifact in **numeric order** (the `--include-optional-all` consumer path)
    with `scripts/scratch-tree.sh`, asserts `release.json.optional_tree`, and builds the **`all` target once**; it
    prints `OPTIONAL_BUILD=PASS`.  **Non-vacuity**: run the PRE-fix artifact through the same scratch path and the
    build must fail (r367: the old `0101` reproduces the reporter's errors; the repaired one builds in 438 s).  The
    artifact's own behaviour gate is separate and still `scripts/gate-mixed-kv.sh` on that build (`BUILD=<scratch>/bin`).
    Required for any re-cut of an artifact, any block amendment that touches a file an artifact patches (the r360
    collision class), and every release (`WORKLOG.md` 261126.005).


**Gate 48 (r371, GitHub issue #377) — the shared-expert down epilogue's rounding.**  `shexp_down_gated_q8_0`
stands in for a `MUL` + two `ADD` nodes, so its epilogue owes three roundings; it had one (HIP contracted
`__fmul_rn(...) + c` into an `fma`, `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §102.2/§112) and the comment claimed otherwise.
`scripts/gate-shexp-down-gate.sh` reads the **emitted ISA** (the build's own `llvm-objcopy` /
`clang-offload-bundler` / `llvm-objdump`, from `CMAKE_HIP_COMPILER`'s directory): for every one of the 32
`shexp_down_gated_q8_0<nwarps, ncols>` instantiations, each epilogue chain that ends in a `global_store_b32` must
have its own `v_mul_f32` and **no** `v_fma_f32`/`v_fmac_f32`/`v_fma_mix_f32` between that product and the store
(the store anchor is the part that matters: a window anchored on the reduction is wrong for `<8,8>`, where the
compiler interleaves the eight-token reduction with the dot loop).  Non-vacuity: 32 instantiations must be found,
the same pattern must match elsewhere in each kernel (the dot products do use fma forms), and the run is repeated
on the pre-fix object, where it reports **144 contracted chains**.  Plus the census leg the arm needs anyway:
`GGML_CUDA_SHEXP_GATE_DEBUG=1` must print at least one `SHEXP DOWN GATE <out> ncols=N exact=1` and
`GGML_CUDA_DISABLE_SHEXP_DOWN_GATE=1` must print **none**.  Why nothing else can judge it: the fusion arms only at
≤ `MMVQ_MAX_BATCH_SIZE` tokens, so greedy text is byte-identical (`2f79e37ca208`, `a2cdef23588f`), the canonical
prefill-logit point reads `0.000000 / 100.000 %`, and a `--chunks 2` log-prob file is byte-identical; at `-ub 1` the
same pair reads 0.001354 KLD / 98.431 %, which is the rounding-level magnitude of the change and its non-vacuity
proof.  Record: `benchmarks/2026-11-27-shexp-down-epilogue-rounding.md`.

Run the GPU gates **alone**: two concurrent GPU jobs change the arena/band layout and invent failures.

on the tip.

## Failure handling (agent instruction)

Apply in the order above, to a branch from `release.json.base`, with `scripts/apply-all.sh`.  If a block
fails to apply:

1. Let the script's `git am -3` retry run; if the merged tree differs from `release.json.tree`, **stop** —
   the checkout was not at the recorded base, or the set is stale.
2. For a true conflict, resolve the hunks **in the block that owns the code** (never in the last block),
   then regenerate the set (`scripts/make-patches.sh`) rather than leaving a hand-edited patch.
3. Do not skip a block.  The series is linear and each block assumes its predecessors; there is no
   supported "apply a subset" flow (including for the k-quant block — see `GREEDY-PURITY.md` §8).
4. Record what happened in `WORKLOG.md`, with the base/tip/tree you ended up with.

## Known notes (working)

- **`test-backend-ops.cpp` is shared by several blocks** (03/04/05/07/08/10/11).  Their hunks sit in different
  case regions, so a re-base is local to the failing file — but grep every patch that touches the file
  before concluding a case is missing.
- **Block 08 is one diff on purpose.**  Its matchers and the `mmvq` / `mmq` / `norm` / `quantize` kernels
  they dispatch to co-developed over one kernel region; do not try to split it.  (The `ggml_cuda_try_fuse`
  translation unit itself is block 10's.)
- **Blocks own their code.**  A fix goes into the block whose surface it changes, even if that means
  amending an early block; appending it to the last block is what made the per-block build gate necessary.
- **The set must apply whitespace-clean.**  `apply-all.sh` prints no git whitespace warnings; a warning is
  a regeneration bug, not a cosmetic issue.
- **Keep UPSTREAM's identifiers in `src/llama-model.{cpp,h}`** — most blocks touch that file, so a local
  rename is paid on every re-base.  Since r128 the loader parameter is `ml` again (the delivery had
  renamed it `ml_l` to silence clang's `-Wshadow`, which re-skinned every conflict in the file — TODO #101);
  the 9 standing `declaration shadows a field` warnings that buys are documented in `src/CMakeLists.txt`
  and are expected.
- **Block-number collisions in old records:** documents written before 2026-10-13 use the
  pre-V2.0 16-block numbering ("block 12" = hybrid all-reduce there, but "block 10" = the k-quant
  umbrella).  `WORKLOG.md`, `archive/` and the dated notes in `archive/docs/patch-notes-history.md` are
  historical and keep their numbering; `patches/README.md` and this file use the current (V2.1) numbers,
  and the block subjects are the table above.  **r109 split the old block 10 in two:** the infrastructure
  stayed block 10 and the qwen4exp architecture became block 11, so an older note that says "block 10" is
  block 10 for slab/dispatcher/async-input and block 11 for qwen4exp/QSA/HC/lazy-reader.

## History

Dated validation records, the retired block-structure churn and the pre-V2.0 per-block verification table:
[`archive/docs/validation-history.md`](archive/docs/validation-history.md).  Baseline/re-base records:
[`archive/docs/baseline-history.md`](archive/docs/baseline-history.md).
34. **A take-over promise lives for ONE staging pass** (r307, block 10, GitHub issue #327) --
    `scripts/gate-moe-promise.sh`.  A promise ("I skip filling `input_cpy`, the consumer reads the arena") was
    identified by the buffer ADDRESS, and the scheduler recycles it: a captured decode graph replays its launches
    without dispatching, so the consumer never runs and the promise is never cleared, and a later wide pass whose
    `pf_in_place_locked` declines then matched that address and re-read every routed table from the host master
    (138 x 206-450 MiB, ~10.4 s, on every 30-34 token prompt).  Pass scoping is now enforced at the END of every
    `graph_compute` (`moe_cache_pass_end`), not at the promise sites.  HARD (`seq` arm, the issue's shape:
    Flash-Next GSQ-RCO IQ3_XXS, one GPU, `-ncmoe 47 --host-experts pool -b/-ub 2048`, one slot, requests in order
    so the short ones follow a decode pass): **no** `moe_cache_repair_copy` line in the whole run; the exit census
    reads `take-over promises broken=0 repaired=0`; and `stale > 0` -- the non-vacuity check, because a pass only
    reaches the repair when the arena is PARTIAL (295 of 512 slots/table on the issue's shape) and an identity
    arena takes the take-over path and serves.  HARD: the short requests really prefill in the 17..64-token band
    (`prompt_n`, not cached) and the repeated request's text is byte-identical.  HARD (`fault` arm, the repair
    itself): `MOE_EXPERT_TAKEOVER_FAULT=1` on an IDENTITY arena (2 devices `-sm layer -ncmoe 48 -c 8192`,
    `prompts/kld-corpus-35b-in-domain.txt`) must still read `broken = n > 0`, `repaired == broken` and `stale == 0`,
    with the no-fault arm's text byte-identical (r298: `595 chars sha=c0e7e91d84c4`) -- the pair that proves the
    pass scoping removed vacuous repairs rather than the repair.  Measured r307: `seq` 0 repairs / `stale=8178` and
    the short prompts 2176 -> 252 ms and 2070 -> 167 ms; `fault` `285/285/0`.  **Negative-tested on the r306
    build**: 3 checks fail (138 repairs, `broken=138 repaired=138`, `stale=0`) while the `fault` arm still passes.
    Required for any change to `taken_cpy`/`moe_cache_pass_end`/`moe_cache_repair_copy`, to a promise site
    (`moe_cache_take_over`, `moe_cache_update_host`'s prefill and eager branches, `moe_cache_remap_dev`), or to
    when the CUDA backend ends a staging pass; every release that touches block 10.
35. **A fused-op probe cannot verify an op its probe graph never built** (r308, block 05, gfx1151 field report) --
    `scripts/gate-fused-probe-splits.sh`.  `resolve_fused_ops()` decides a fused feature from where the probe
    graph's node LANDED, so the probe must CONTAIN the node (`require_observed`); the reserve/probe context carries
    `n_kv = kv->get_size()` (the whole padded cache extent), and qwen4exp's QSA decode arm is dense only while
    `n_kv < qsa_dense_decode_until` (32768 on gfx1151), so at `-c >= 32768` the kv_rows probe built the SPARSE arm
    and reported `enabled` having verified nothing -- and at the time no AMD kernel read the rows form, so every
    joint attention layer ran on the CPU (25 splits, plain 32.5 -> 11.0 t/s, MTP n3 18.7 t/s).  HARD, six arms
    (`--kv-unified -c 32768 -np 2`, one active slot): **no** arm logs `Flash Attention over K/V rows is assigned to
    device CPU` and **no** `sched_graph_realloc` line reports `splits` above the LAYOUT's bound; `sparse` must
    decline BY OBSERVATION (`was not used in the probe graph`), `dense-always` (`LLAMA_QSA_DENSE_DECODE_UNTIL=1e9`,
    the gfx1100/gfx1201 policy) and `mtp-rdna34` BY PLACEMENT **or read `enabled`** -- since r358 an AMD MMA kernel
    (MFMA/WMMA with the GQA-optimised layout) really does read the rows form, so `enabled` is the correct verdict on
    such a box and the defect this gate hunts is a CPU placement, never the verdict alone (`KVROWS_EXPECT` pins the
    box); the arms that decode the same rows regime read byte-identical greedy text (`plain == dense-always`, `mtp ==
    mtp-rdna34`, and `plain == rows-off` on a box that cannot read the rows form); `rows-off`
    (`LLAMA_KV_ROWS_DISABLE=1`) is the control.  **Why the MTP
    arms are mandatory:** the verify band takes the same dense arm as W=1 and the drafter has its OWN unified
    context -- and the MTP arm is what caught the first cut of this fix, which initialized `n_seqs` instead of
    `require_observed` (the initializers are positional; `INVARIANTS.md` r308 item 5).  **Negative test:** `--log`
    on r307's decisive lines (a `Flash Attention over K/V rows is assigned to device CPU` placement, `splits = 25`)
    FAILs both assertions -- and since r358 the third one is a bare, verdict-stripped `enabled` for an audited
    probe.
    Measured r308: `splits = 1` on every arm, plain 32.5 t/s (== `--no-kv-unified`), MTP n3 62.4-65.0 t/s, and the
    non-QSA arm (27B 21.9 / 35B-A3B 90.2 t/s, embedded MTP) reads the placement decline, i.e. unaffected.
    **(r359, issue #365) the rows form is OPT-IN, so the gate also pins the DEFAULT.**  A new `default` arm runs with
    no env at all and must log `K/V rows attention: dense form`, must never log a rows-form claim, and must read the
    same greedy text as `rows-off`; every arm that exercises the form now sets `LLAMA_KV_ROWS_DISABLE=0`, so
    `rows-off` is the same default spelled out.  Why: on gfx1201 the form cost 17-59 % of decode at 90k depth and it
    silently disabled the derived (materialised-mask-free) kq mask -- `INVARIANTS.md` r359.  Measured r359 (same box
    and shape as r358): 7 arms, `default`/`rows-off` both `f6f0a8fd998b` (the r311/r358 sha), the four opt-in arms
    `enabled`, `sparse` by observation, every graph `splits = 4`, no CPU placement, no GPU fault.
    Required for any change to a probe descriptor, `resolve_fused_ops()`, or a `cparams.fused_*` enable decision.

    **(r311, `TODO` #345) the same gate, extended to the vacuity CLASS.**  The r308 arms above grade ONE probe on
    ONE box; two of them (`plain`, `mtp`) are box-dependent by construction (they leave
    `LLAMA_QSA_DENSE_DECODE_UNTIL` at the box default: SPARSE on gfx1151, DENSE on gfx1201), so `KVROWS_EXPECT=enable|obs|place`
    pins them and the default derives them.  The class-wide instrument is the **fixture ladder**:
    `test-llama-archs -a <arch> -v 4` per arch (`qwen4exp deepseek4 deepseek41 glm5-next kimi-k31 deepseek32 dots3note
    glm-dsa mistral-large4`; ~2.4 s each, no model load, a real context so `resolve_fused_ops()` runs) asserting the
    recorded per-(arch, probe) `obs`/`query` table `PROBE_AUDIT`; `--fixture-only` runs just that.  Plus: a bare
    `fused <probe> enabled` FAILS for `DSV4 HC pre`/`comb`/`post` and `fused top-k mask` (each must carry its verdict
    marker), `DSV4_HC_POST` is pinned on any arm that builds a Meta device (it is `meta_split_active`-gated), and
    `splits` is bounded by the LAYOUT (`SPLITS_MAX`, 1 single-device/tensor and NGPU+1 on a layer split -- the output
    layer is placed back on the first device, so 3 GPUs read 4).  **Negative-tested three ways** (stripped verdict,
    a `kv_rows` CPU placement, `splits = 25`), and the pre-fix r310 binary FAILS on the real Flash-Next shape.  Recorded
    reachability: see `INVARIANTS.md` r311 item 1 (`deepseek41`'s top-k mask is `query` at 1 token although its
    compressed-attention path builds it at runtime -- the row that proves `require_observed` would have been a
    REGRESSION there).  Uncovered arches: `xing4-0`, `hy-v4` (no fixture, no GGUF).
